# Requirements

Owner: planner. Ids are never renumbered or reused. Format per entry:
`REQ-### | title | spec ref | statement | acceptance criteria (observable, testable) | area`.

Spec line numbers (L…) refer to `docs/spec/Technical_Specification.md` as of 2026-10-19. "Planted" means a violation added in a throwaway copy or branch by the tester, never committed.

## M0 scaffolding (done)

Scope: manager, agent and web trees that do nothing yet, with every CI gate in `.claude/CLAUDE.md` "Commands" and `.claude/rules/testing.md` "Static gates" running and able to fail. Nothing from the CLAUDE.md out-of-scope list is planned.

| REQ-010 | Go module and three static agent binaries | TS §2.1 (L107), TS §3.1 (L158), TS §8.2 (L1265 "no CGO"); CLAUDE.md layout `agent/cmd/…` | `agent/` is one Go module (path and Go version PD-14 (a), (b)). `agent/cmd/bitscan-agent`, `bitscan-runner` and `bitscan-capread` build as static Linux binaries with `CGO_ENABLED=0` for amd64 and arm64. (2) `go version -m <binary>` shows `CGO_ENABLED=0`, `GOOS=linux` and the matching `GOARCH`; on Linux, `file` reports each as statically linked. (3) No agent package has a cgo file (`CgoFiles`), a hand-written assembly file (`SFiles`, `.s`) or a prebuilt object file (`SysoFiles`, `.syso`). None of these is checked by gofmt, golangci-lint or the REQ-012 guards. Also, with `CGO_ENABLED=0`, a file with `import "C"` is silently left out of the build, so the build still exits 0 unless that file is the package's only one. So the check is a listing, run from `agent/` for amd64 and arm64, that must print nothing: `CGO_ENABLED=1 GOOS=linux go list -f '{{if or .CgoFiles .SFiles .SysoFiles}}{{.ImportPath}}: cgo={{.CgoFiles}} s={{.SFiles}} syso={{.SysoFiles}}{{end}}' ./...`. Both architectures are listed because file-name suffixes such as `_amd64.s` select files per `GOARCH`. Each of these planted files makes the listing print the package and the CI step exit non-zero: (a) a file with `import "C"` next to other files in an agent package, even though the `CGO_ENABLED=0` build still exits 0; (b) `raw_amd64.s` next to `agent/cmd/bitscan-agent/main.go`, implementing a body-less Go func declared in that package; (c) a `.syso` file in an agent package. (4) On Linux, each binary exits non-zero within 1 s, writes nothing to stdout and one "not implemented" line to stderr. | agent |

## Not covered in M0 (by design)

Every other TS and SR section is planned in later milestones (new order in `work/decisions.md`, 2026-10-20). See the coverage matrices in `work/tasks.md`.

## M1 database and audit core

Scope, confirmed by the user on 2026-10-20 (`work/decisions.md`, PD-18 and M1 scope rows):
- the TS §5.2 schema as several forward-only Alembic migrations that together form the initial set, with the seeds and `scan_result` partitions in the last one;
- the TS §8.6 start-up configuration loader;
- `bitscan-manager db migrate` and `db maintain`;
- Debian version comparison tested against `dpkg`;
- the audit hash chain core (append and verify; the sink and disk queue are M2);
- the test-harness carry-overs from M0.

`serve`, `users …` and `bits rebuild` stay M0 stubs. Spec line numbers (L…) refer to `docs/spec/Technical_Specification.md`. Open questions #38 to #46 are in `work/open-questions.md`.

"Grant probe" means the statement is run on a freshly migrated database over a connection whose `session_user` is the named role, and the result is checked: either it succeeds, or it fails with SQLSTATE 42501 (`insufficient_privilege`). "Diff-equal" means the REQ-027 schema comparison shows no difference for the named objects (method: #40).

| REQ | Title | Spec ref | Statement | Acceptance criteria | Area |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| REQ-018 | Runtime database dependencies, hash-pinned | TS §5.3 (L1046 "Alembic"), TS §8.2 (L1264), TS §8.1 "Install and supply chain" (L1258); PD-14 (i); decisions 2026-10-20 (final lock command) | The runtime lock adds Alembic, SQLAlchemy (Alembic's engine) and psycopg 3 with a libpq that supports TLS. Both locks are regenerated with the recorded lock command and still install with `--require-hashes`. | (1) `pip install --require-hashes -r manager/requirements.txt` exits 0 in a fresh CPython 3.12 venv on Windows and on Linux (CI; x86_64 and aarch64 via the `uv --python-platform` proxy as in M0). The same holds for `manager/requirements-dev.txt`. (2) After the runtime install, `python -c "import alembic, sqlalchemy, psycopg"` exits 0. (3) The runtime lock still contains no test or lint tool (`pytest`, `ruff`, `mypy`, `testcontainers`). (4) Regenerating with the lock command and cutoff in the lock header reproduces both files byte for byte. (5) `mypy --strict --config-file manager/pyproject.toml manager` still exits 0. | manager |
| REQ-019 | Start-up configuration loader | TS §8.6 (L1302–L1315), TS §8.7 (L1345, L1362–L1371, L1414–L1416, L1447–L1449), TS §8.2 (L1269), TS §5.3 (L1049 "over TLS"), TS §8.1 "Secrets in config" (L1259), TS §8.4 (L1287); question #24, #45 | `bitscan_manager.config` loads start-up configuration from environment variables and the files they point to. There are two profiles. The **serve** profile needs the eight required TS §8.6 variables. The **db** profile, used by `db migrate` and `db maintain`, needs only `BITSCAN_MIGRATE_DSN_FILE`: the TS §8.7 migrate and maintain units set nothing else. Secrets come only from `*_FILE` paths, and no secret is ever printed or logged. In M1 nothing wires the serve profile to `serve`, which stays a stub; M2 does that. | (1) Serve profile, with all of `BITSCAN_DB_DSN_FILE`, `BITSCAN_RETENTION_DSN_FILE`, `BITSCAN_VAULT_ADDR`, `BITSCAN_TOTP_KEY_REF`, `BITSCAN_VAULT_TOKEN_FILE`, `BITSCAN_TLS_CERT_FILE`, `BITSCAN_TLS_KEY_FILE` and `BITSCAN_AUDIT_SINK` set to valid values: loading succeeds. The DSNs and vault token are read from their files. The listen address defaults to `0.0.0.0:8443`, the bits folder to `/var/lib/bitscan/bits`, and the upload folder is `<bits folder>/uploaded`. (2) For each of the eight, when the variable is unset or empty, loading raises a configuration error whose message names that variable. (3) When a `*_FILE` variable points to a missing, unreadable or empty file, the error names the variable, not the file content. (4) Plain-value look-alikes (`BITSCAN_DB_DSN`, `BITSCAN_RETENTION_DSN`, `BITSCAN_VAULT_TOKEN`, `BITSCAN_MIGRATE_DSN`) are never read. Setting one without its `_FILE` partner still fails as "missing `…_FILE`". (5) An app DSN that does not require TLS is refused, and the error names `BITSCAN_DB_DSN_FILE`. That covers no `sslmode`, and `sslmode` = `disable`, `allow` or `prefer`. Which `sslmode` values count as TLS, and whether the retention and migrate DSNs get the same check, follow #45. An unparsable DSN is refused, and neither the error message nor its exception chain contains any part of the DSN. (6) The serve profile never reads `BITSCAN_MIGRATE_DSN_FILE`: with it set to an unreadable path, loading still succeeds and that key is never looked up. (7) The db profile succeeds with only `BITSCAN_MIGRATE_DSN_FILE` set, and fails naming it when it is missing. (8) No secret leaks. Put a canary password in every DSN file and a canary in the vault-token file. On every success and failure path, neither `repr()` nor `str()` of the configuration objects or errors, nor anything written to stdout, stderr or a log record, contains a canary. (9) `BITSCAN_VAULT_ADDR`, `BITSCAN_TOTP_KEY_REF` and `BITSCAN_AUDIT_SINK` are kept as opaque non-empty strings. They are not parsed for a scheme, and no connection is made (#24). (10) Loading opens no socket and no database connection. (11) A `BITSCAN_LISTEN` that is not `host:port` (or `[v6]:port`) with a port from 1 to 65535 is refused, and the error names the variable. | manager |
| REQ-020 | Migration runner and `bitscan-manager db migrate` | TS §5.3 (L1046, L1049), TS §8.6 (L1308), TS §8.7 (L1346, L1405–L1435); testing.md "Static gates" (forward-only) and L51; REQ-008 (narrowed) | `bitscan-manager db migrate` loads the db profile (REQ-019) and connects with the `bitscan_migrate` DSN. It runs `SET ROLE bitscan_owner` and upgrades Alembic to head. Migrations are hand-written SQL run through Alembic `op.execute` (or bound-parameter statements for data), and they are forward-only. | (1) On a database prepared by only the TS §5.2 Bootstrap block, `bitscan-manager db migrate` with `BITSCAN_MIGRATE_DSN_FILE` set exits 0, and the Alembic revision is head. (2) Every table, partitioned table, partition, index, sequence, type, function and view in schema `public`, including Alembic's version table (#41), is owned by `bitscan_owner`. None is owned by `bitscan_migrate`. (3) A second run exits 0 and changes nothing: catalog snapshots taken before and after are equal. (4) Forward-only: calling any migration's `downgrade()` raises, and `bitscan-manager db downgrade` (or any other `db` subcommand except `migrate` and `maintain`) exits non-zero and changes nothing. (5) With a DSN for a role that is not a member of `bitscan_owner` (for example `bitscan_app`), the command exits non-zero and creates nothing. (6) With `BITSCAN_MIGRATE_DSN_FILE` unset, it exits non-zero, and stderr names the variable. No canary password from the DSN file appears in stdout or stderr on any path, including a wrong password and an unreachable host. (7) `serve`, `users …`, `bits rebuild` and unknown commands keep the M0 behaviour. They exit non-zero, read no `BITSCAN_*` variable and import no database module (`psycopg`, `sqlalchemy`, `alembic`). (8) It works from a non-editable install of the wheel, so the migration files ship in the package. (9) `ruff check manager` passes with rule S608 active: no SQL is built with f-strings, `%` or `.format`. | manager |
| REQ-021 | Schema part 1: catalog | TS §5.2 (L638–L707); TS §5.1 (L625–L629) | Migration `m0001` creates the three extensions (no-ops after the Bootstrap), the types `policy_status`, `plugin_state` and `plugin_operator`, and the tables `family`, `policy`, `plugin`, `policy_plugin` and `bit_import`, exactly as TS §5.2. | (1) These objects are diff-equal to TS §5.2. (4) Defaults: `policy.status_overridden` false, `format_version` `'1.0'`, `version` 1; `plugin.state` `active`; `bit_import.errors` `[]`. | REQ-029 | Debian version comparison through `debversion` | TS §7.2 (L1219), TS §8.3 Unit (L1275 "version compare"), TS §5.2 (L804); testing.md Unit | Package versions stay text and are compared with `version::debversion`, using `ix_package_name_debver`. (3) On the migrated database, `a::debversion` compared with `b::debversion` gives the recorded relation for every pair. Until `m0005` exists, this runs as `bitscan_owner` (via `bitscan_migrate`), because `bitscan_app` has no table privileges before the grants. From TASK-018 on, it runs as `bitscan_app`. | REQ-030 | Audit chain append | TS §5.2 (L981–L990), TS §5.3 (L1049, L1051), TS §8.1 (L1240, L1253); security invariants "Data handling"; question #38 | `services/audit` appends one `audit_log` entry inside the caller's transaction. It takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('audit_log'))`, sets `prev_hash` to the latest entry's `row_hash` (or the genesis value, #38) and `row_hash` to the SHA-256 of `prev_hash` and the entry (exact input #38), and inserts with parameterised SQL. In M1 nothing leaves the database: the sink, disk queue and alerts are M2. | (1) After N appends, every entry's `prev_hash` equals the previous entry's `row_hash`, ordered by `audit_id`. The first entry's `prev_hash` is the genesis value. Recomputing each `row_hash` from the stored row (#38) gives the stored value. (2) Eight concurrent writers, each on its own `bitscan_app` connection, append 25 entries each. The result is one unbroken chain of 200 entries, and no two entries share a `prev_hash`. (3) While one transaction holds an uncommitted append, a second append on another connection waits on an advisory lock (seen in `pg_locks`) until the first commits or rolls back. After a rollback, the second entry links to the last committed entry. (4) Append works as `bitscan_app` and as `bitscan_retention` (for checkpoint entries) and needs no UPDATE or DELETE privilege. (5) A writer in REPEATABLE READ or SERIALIZABLE, or in autocommit mode, never forks the chain: it either appends correctly or raises before inserting. (6) Values longer than the column limits (`actor` 128, `action` 64, `target` 255) are rejected and nothing is inserted. (7) Append writes nothing to application logs, stdout or stderr, and opens no network connection. (8) The hash function is pure, and its unit tests run without PostgreSQL, so the coverage gate on `services/` holds on the Windows dev box. | manager |
| REQ-031 | Audit chain verifier | TS §5.3 (L1051 "so the chain can still be verified from that point"); audit-and-alerts.md Tests; question #39 | `services/audit` verifies the stored chain. It reads the entries in `audit_id` order from one snapshot, recomputes every `row_hash`, checks every link, and accepts a start after deleted entries only where an `audit.checkpoint` entry covers it (#39). It reports the first failing `audit_id`. | (1) An intact chain verifies, and the number of entries checked is reported. An empty log verifies. (2) As `bitscan_owner` (via `bitscan_migrate`), changing any hashed field of one entry makes the verifier report that entry's `audit_id`. (3) Deleting a middle entry reports the entry after the gap. Inserting an entry whose `prev_hash` is not the previous entry's `row_hash` reports that entry. (4) Gaps in `audit_id` from rolled-back appends do not fail verification when the links are intact. (5) Across a checkpoint: as `bitscan_retention`, write an `audit.checkpoint` entry with the id and `row_hash` of the last entry to delete (#39), then delete the entries up to that id. The chain still verifies. Deleting one more entry, or changing the checkpoint's recorded hash, makes it fail. (6) The verification logic is pure over a sequence of rows and is unit-tested without PostgreSQL. | manager |
| REQ-032 | PostgreSQL test harness on the app roles | testing.md L51 ("run the TS §5.2 Bootstrap block, then `bitscan-manager db migrate` … Tests never run as a superuser after the bootstrap"); TS §5.2 (L1016–L1033); TS §8.3 Integration (L1278); REQ-009 (4); decisions 2026-10-20 (carry-overs) | The tester's harness runs the TS §5.2 Bootstrap block verbatim, then `bitscan-manager db migrate` as `bitscan_migrate`. It hands tests connections as `bitscan_app`, `bitscan_retention` and `bitscan_migrate` only, and binds the container port to localhost. The M0 skip/exit hook gets its own pytester test. | (1) The Bootstrap SQL is taken at test time from the TS §5.2 block (L1018–L1033), so a change to the spec shows up, and `\c bitscan` is carried out as a reconnect to `bitscan`. The only other superuser statements are test-only role passwords and the container's TLS set-up, each listed in `tests/m1/test-plan.md`. (2) `db migrate` is run through the CLI with `BITSCAN_MIGRATE_DSN_FILE` pointing to a temporary file. (3) Every connection the fixtures hand out has `session_user` in {`bitscan_app`, `bitscan_retention`, `bitscan_migrate`}, and `rolsuper` is false for it. (4) `docker inspect` shows the PostgreSQL port bound to `127.0.0.1` only. (5) The container runs with TLS on, so DSNs with `sslmode=require` connect (REQ-019 (5)). (6) Pytester: with Docker unreachable and `CI` unset, `pg` tests are skipped with "not run: Docker unavailable" and the other tests still run. With `CI=true`, the session exits non-zero with that message. With no `pg` test selected, Docker is not probed. | manager |
| REQ-033 | CI gate: migrations are forward-only, one per PR | testing.md "Static gates" ("Migrations are forward-only, one per PR"), TS §5.3 (L1046) | On every pull request, the manager workflow fails when an existing migration file is changed, or when more than one migration file is added. The PostgreSQL suites run with `CI=true`. | (1) A PR that modifies, deletes or renames a file under `manager/bitscan_manager/db/migrations/versions/` (other than `__init__.py`, which is created once) fails the job. (2) A PR that adds two migration files fails; one that adds zero or one passes. (3) The PostgreSQL test job runs with `CI=true`, so a missing Docker fails it, and the M1 suites (REQ-020 to REQ-031) are among the tests it runs. (4) The step has no `continue-on-error`, `\|\| true` or skip condition. | deploy |

### Changes to M0 requirements

- REQ-008 (2) is narrowed. From TASK-013, `db migrate` is a real command, and from TASK-019, so is `db maintain`. Every other invocation (`serve`, `users …`, `bits rebuild`, unknown) keeps the M0 behaviour: exit non-zero, read no `BITSCAN_*` variable, touch no database. The tester updates `manager/tests/test_cli.py` in TASK-013 TEST.
- REQ-009 (4) gets the pytester test it lacked (REQ-032 (6)).

## M2 auth core and API plumbing

Scope, from `work/m2-brief.md` and the milestone reorder (`work/decisions.md`, 2026-10-20):
- users and roles; Argon2id passwords; sessions; TOTP enrolment and verification;
- RBAC and the TOTP-verified-session check for sensitive actions; `bsu_` and `bsk_` tokens;
- the audit sink with its disk queue, the outage alert and the 15-minute refusal; the alert delivery path;
- vault access for the TOTP key;
- security headers, request-size limits and rate limits; problem details, pagination and JSON logs;
- `/healthz` and `/readyz`; OpenTelemetry explicitly off in `create_app()`;
- `bitscan-manager serve`, `users create-admin`, `users create-api-token`, and `users reset-totp` (added by the planner: TS §6.2 L1090 and §8.7 L1347 list it next to the other two; the lead may defer it to M3);
- the web `baseUrl` recheck.

Not in M2: agent routes, `bse_`/`bsa_` tokens, enrolment, the scheduler and inventory (M3); `GET`/`PATCH /settings` and `GET /audit-log` (proposed for M3, see the coverage matrix in `work/tasks.md`); the Prometheus metrics route (#5); UI screens (M5); anything in the CLAUDE.md out-of-scope list.

**Migrations: none.** Every column M2 uses exists in TS §5.2: `app_user` (L953–L964), `user_token` (L966–L979), `audit_log` (L981–L990), `system_schedule` (L827–L831) and Alembic's version table (#41). If an answer to #7 (a forced password change) or #49 (a stored pending TOTP seed) needed a new column, that would be a schema change and goes back to the owner; the recommended defaults need none.

Conventions for this section. L… are lines of `docs/spec/Technical_Specification.md`. A "user route" is any `/api/v1` route outside `/api/v1/agent/`. A "TOTP session" is a `bsu_` session row with `totp_verified = true`. "Refused" means the request changes nothing, writes no audit entry other than any one the criterion names, and gets the status and problem `type` fixed by #48; until #48 is answered, tests assert the status class given in the criterion (401, 403, 409, 429 or 503). A "canary" is a unique random string planted as a password, token, TOTP seed, DSN password or vault token; a canary sweep searches captured log records, stdout, stderr, response bodies and exception text (including chains) for it. "Fake clock" means the `Clock` from TASK-025, which every time-based rule reads.

(2) After the runtime install, importing each new package exits 0. (3) The runtime lock contains no test or lint tool (`pytest`, `ruff`, `mypy`, `testcontainers`). (4) Regenerating with the lock command and the cutoff in the lock header reproduces both files byte for byte; a moved cutoff is a reviewed dependency update. (5) Neither lock contains an OpenTelemetry SDK or exporter package (`opentelemetry-sdk`, `opentelemetry-exporter-*`), nor an OIDC or identity-provider library. | manager |

It still serves no `/docs`, `/redoc` or `/openapi.json` (#34) and adds no CORS middleware. Every user route is registered with the full `/api/v1` prefix, so the OpenAPI paths carry it. | (1) With `FASTAPI_OTEL_AUTO_CONFIGURE=true`, `OTEL_TRACES_EXPORTER=otlp`, `OTEL_METRICS_EXPORTER=otlp`, `OTEL_LOGS_EXPORTER=otlp` and `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` pointing at a local TCP listener, 20 requests to the app cause no connection to that listener within 5 s, and no `opentelemetry.sdk` module is imported. (2) The same holds with those variables unset. (3) `GET /docs`, `/docs/oauth2-redirect`, `/redoc` and `/openapi.json` get 404. (4) A CORS preflight (`OPTIONS`, `Origin: https://evil.example`, `Access-Control-Request-Method: POST`) on any route, including `/api/v1/auth/login`, gets no `Access-Control-Allow-*` header. (5) In the exported `manager/openapi.json`, every user-route path starts with `/api/v1/`. | manager |
| REQ-036 | Security headers on every response | TS §6.3 (L1140), TS §8.1 (L1254); #57 | Every response carries HSTS (value: #57), `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'self'`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`. | (1) Responses with status 200, 204, 401, 403, 404, 405, 413, 422, 429, 500 (an unhandled exception in a test route) and 503 each carry all four headers, the CSP byte for byte as above, including on `/healthz` and `/readyz`. | REQ-037 | Request body size limit | TS §6.3 (L1138), TS §8.1 (L1250); #57 | Request bodies on every route are capped at 1 MB ("other JSON bodies"; byte value: #57) unless a route registers one of the TS §6.3 caps for its class (inventory, result batch, `.bit`; those routes arrive in M3 and M7). A larger body gets 413 before it is parsed. | (1) A body of exactly the cap is passed on; one byte more gets 413. (2) With a `Content-Length` over the cap, 413 is returned and at most cap + 1 bytes of the body are read. (3) A chunked body with no `Content-Length` that exceeds the cap gets 413, and reading stops after cap + 1 bytes. (4) The 413 body is problem details (REQ-038) and carries the REQ-036 headers. (5) The cap registry accepts only the TS §6.3 values: registering a cap above the value for its class raises at app creation. | manager |
| REQ-038 | Problem details, cursor pagination, timestamps and ids | TS §6.3 (L1133, L1134, L1139), TS §7.1 (L1212, L1215); #47, #48, #52 | Every error response is RFC 9457 problem details. Lists use cursor pagination (`?limit=50&cursor=<opaque>`, at most 500), with the response shape of TS §7.1 L1215 (`{ "items": [...], "next_cursor": ... }`, adopted for every list per #47). Timestamps are RFC 3339 UTC; UUID ids are strings, and BIGSERIAL ids follow #52. | (1) Every 4xx and 5xx from M2 routes, including request-validation errors, unknown paths, wrong methods, 413 and unhandled exceptions, has `Content-Type: application/problem+json` and a body with exactly the keys `type`, `title`, `status`, `detail` and `instance`; `status` equals the HTTP status and `instance` is the request path without its query string. (2) No error body contains a stack trace, SQL text, a DSN, a token or a password (canary sweep). (3) A validation error names the field, never its value, and uses the status fixed by #48 (TS §7.1 L1212 uses 400 for bad query parameters). (4) `limit` defaults to 50 and accepts 1–500; 0, 501 and non-integers are refused with the #48 status. Paging through N + 1 items with `limit=N` returns every item exactly once, in a stable order, and `next_cursor` is null on the last page. A tampered or foreign cursor is refused with the #48 status. (5) Every timestamp in a response body ends in `Z` or `+00:00` and parses as RFC 3339. | manager |
| REQ-039 | Structured JSON logs, no secrets | TS §8.4 (L1287), TS §5.3 (L1052), TS §8.1 (L1249, L1255); security invariants "Secrets and output" | The manager logs JSON lines carrying `request_id` (new for each request), `agent_id` and `job_id` (null in M2). Tokens, passwords, TOTP codes and seeds, DSNs, the vault token, `Authorization` headers, query strings and request bodies are never logged. | (1) Each request produces at least one log record that `json.loads` parses, with the keys `request_id`, `agent_id` and `job_id`; `request_id` is the same across one request's records and differs between requests. (2) Canary sweep over a run that signs in, fails sign-ins, enrols TOTP, creates and uses API tokens, creates users, runs `serve` and the three CLI commands: no canary appears in any captured log record, stdout or stderr. (3) Access records hold method, path without the query string, status and duration, and no header values. (4) An unhandled exception is logged with its type and `request_id`, without request bodies or local variables. | manager |
| REQ-040 | User token format and storage | TS §6.2 (L1083), TS §2.3 (L122–L123), TS §5.2 (L971); security invariants "Secrets and output"; #56 | `bsu_` and `bsk_` tokens are the prefix followed by the base64url encoding of 32 bytes from the operating system's CSPRNG (padding: #56). Only the lowercase hex SHA-256 of the whole token string is stored, in `user_token.token_hash`. | (1) 10,000 generated tokens of each kind are distinct, start with their prefix, and their body decodes as base64url to exactly 32 bytes; length and padding per #56. (2) The stored `token_hash` equals the SHA-256 hex digest of the token string, lowercase, 64 characters. (3) The token is returned exactly once (the creating response or CLI stdout); no route returns it, or its hash, again. (4) A token whose hash matches a row of the other kind (a `bsu_` string on an `api` row, or the reverse) is refused (401). (5) The DB never holds the raw token: after creating 50 tokens, no text column of `user_token`, `app_user` or `audit_log` contains any of them. | manager |
| REQ-041 | Argon2id passwords | TS §6.2 (L1087), TS §5.2 (L956); #56 | Passwords are stored as Argon2id hashes (parameters: #56, PD-19 library defaults until answered) and must be at least 12 characters wherever a password is set. | (1) A stored `password_hash` is an Argon2id PHC string starting `$argon2id$`. (2) An 11-character password is refused at every place a password is set (`POST /users`, `POST /auth/password`, `users create-admin`); 12 characters are accepted; characters are counted as Unicode code points; the upper bound and normalisation follow #56. (3) Verification succeeds only for the exact password. (4) Two hashes of the same password differ (random salt). (5) No response, log record or audit `detail` contains a password or a password hash (canary sweep). | manager |
| REQ-042 | TOTP verification, each code used once | TS §6.2 (L1087, L1089), TS §5.2 (L957–L958); #49 | TOTP codes follow RFC 6238 with the algorithm, digits, period and accepted window of #49. A code is accepted only if its time step is newer than `app_user.totp_last_step`, and the accepting step is stored atomically with the check. | (1) The RFC 6238 Appendix B test vectors for the #49 algorithm give the stated codes. (2) A correct code inside the window is accepted once; the same code again is refused; a code for a step older than the last accepted step is refused. (3) PostgreSQL: two concurrent sign-ins with the same valid code on two connections give exactly one success. (4) A code outside the window, of the wrong length or with non-digits is refused. (5) Codes are compared in constant time. | manager |
| REQ-043 | TOTP seeds encrypted with the vault key | TS §8.6 (L1310–L1312), TS §5.2 (L957), TS §8.7 (L1345, L1366, L1368), TS §8.1 (L1259); #24, #58 | The manager encrypts and decrypts TOTP seeds with the key named by `BITSCAN_TOTP_KEY_REF` in the vault at `BITSCAN_VAULT_ADDR`, authenticating with the token from `BITSCAN_VAULT_TOKEN_FILE`. `app_user.totp_secret_enc` holds only ciphertext. Vault product and API: #24. Behaviour when the vault is unreachable: #58. | (1) Against a fake vault, an enrolment stores a `totp_secret_enc` that contains neither the base32 seed nor its raw bytes in any encoding, and a later sign-in decrypts it and verifies a code. (2) The vault is called over HTTPS with certificate verification; a fake vault with an untrusted certificate is refused and nothing is stored. (3) The vault token appears only in the request to the vault (canary sweep over logs, errors, exception chains and the `repr` of client objects). (4) Vault errors (unreachable, 403, 5xx, malformed reply) give the #58 behaviour and reveal neither the seed, the vault token nor the vault's response body. | manager |
| REQ-044 | Bearer tokens on user routes | TS §2.3 (L118, L122–L123), TS §6 (L1056), TS §6.2 (L1076, L1080–L1081, L1091), TS §8.3 Security (L1283); security invariants "Authorisation" | Every user route except `POST /auth/login` needs `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. Only `bsu_` and `bsk_` tokens are accepted. The token's row must match its prefix, be unrevoked and unexpired, (for sessions) have been used within the last 8 h, and belong to an enabled user. The request acts with the user's current role, read at each request. | (1) A missing header, another scheme, an empty token, an unknown token, a `bsa_` or `bse_` token (even one whose SHA-256 is present in `agent.token_hash` or `enrollment_token.token_hash`) and an unknown prefix each get 401 on every M2 user route. (2) Fake clock: a session is accepted 7 h 59 min after its last use and refused 8 h after it; a session used every hour is refused 24 h after it was issued. (3) An API token is refused after its `expires_at` and after `revoked_at` is set. (4) After a user is disabled, every token of theirs is refused. (5) After a viewer is promoted to operator, their existing API token passes an operator role check on the next request (REQ-045 test route). (6) Each accepted request updates `last_used_at`, so the idle limit counts from the last use. | manager |
| REQ-045 | Role checks from the TS §6.2 route table | TS §6.2 (L1093–L1127), TS §8.1 (L1253), TS §5.2 (L951) | Roles are ordered `viewer` < `operator` < `admin`. Each user route requires the minimum role in the TS §6.2 table. M2 routes: `POST /auth/login` and `/auth/logout` none; `/auth/totp/enrol`, `/auth/password` any signed-in user; `/api-tokens` viewer; `/users` admin. | (1) For every M2 route, a token of each lower role is refused (403) and the minimum role and above are not refused by the role check. (2) A test that walks every route of `create_app()` finds that each user route either declares a role requirement or is `POST /auth/login`; health routes are outside the walk (#5). (3) The role is read from `app_user` at each request, never from a value stored with the token. | manager |
| REQ-046 | Session-only routes, TOTP-session routes and the restricted admin session | TS §6.2 (L1084, L1085, L1089, L1096–L1098, L1125), TS §8.1 (L1242, L1253), TS §8.3 Security (L1283); security invariants "Authorisation"; #1, #48, #53 | (a) Session-only routes refuse `bsk_` tokens: `/auth/totp/enrol`, `/auth/password`, `POST /api-tokens`. (b) TOTP-session routes need an admin `bsu_` session with `totp_verified = true` and refuse `bsk_`: in M2, every method of `/users`. One reusable dependency implements this, so the later TS §6.2 "TOTP session" routes (including `DELETE /policies/{id}`, #1) use the same check. (c) TOTP-session routes are also refused while the audit-sink gate refuses (REQ-054). (d) An admin without TOTP signs in to a session that can call only `/auth/totp/enrol` and `/auth/logout`. (e) `POST /api-tokens` by an admin needs a TOTP session. | (1) An admin `bsk_` token on each `/users` method is refused (403) with no change, even while the same admin holds a TOTP session. (2) A `bsk_` token of any role on `/auth/totp/enrol`, `/auth/password` and `POST /api-tokens` is refused (403) with no change. (3) An admin session with `totp_verified = false` on `/users` and on `POST /api-tokens` is refused (403). (4) An admin without TOTP: every M2 route other than `/auth/totp/enrol` and `/auth/logout` is refused (403), including `GET /api-tokens`; after enrolment the re-issued session can call `/users`. (5) Operator and viewer sessions on `/users` are refused (403). (6) A route-walk test finds the TOTP-session dependency on every `/users` route. | manager |
| REQ-047 | Rate limits on user routes | TS §6.3 (L1136), TS §8.1 (L1248, L1253); #54, #55 | `POST /auth/login`: 10 requests per minute per source IP. User routes: 600 requests per minute per user. Failed token checks on any route: 20 per minute per source IP, counting and blocking only failed attempts. Failed checks are written to `audit_log` as one aggregated entry per IP per minute. State is in memory (one manager instance, TS §2.1 L103). The source IP is the TCP peer address (#55). | (1) Fake clock: the 11th sign-in attempt from one IP within a minute gets 429 (problem details); another IP is not affected; in the next window it is accepted again. (2) The 601st request of one user within a minute gets 429, counted across all of that user's tokens; another user is not affected. (3) After 20 failed token checks from one IP within a minute, further requests from it with bad tokens get 429, while requests with valid tokens from the same IP still succeed. (4) For each IP and minute with at least one failed check, exactly one audit entry is written with that count (action and `detail` keys: #50); none is written for a minute without failures; the entry is written even if no further request arrives (flushed by `serve`, REQ-057). (5) `X-Forwarded-For` and `Forwarded` headers do not change the IP used (#55). (6) The repeated-401 alert follows #54. (7) Counters for a key are dropped once its window has passed. | manager |
| REQ-048 | Sign-in, back-off and sign-out | TS §6.2 (L1080, L1086–L1089), TS §2.3 (L122), TS §8.1 (L1242); #47, #48, #49, #50, #54, #55 | `POST /api/v1/auth/login` with username, password and (for an admin with TOTP) a code returns a new `bsu_` session: `expires_at` = issue time + 24 h, idle limit 8 h. An admin with TOTP gets `totp_verified = true`; an admin without TOTP gets the restricted session of REQ-046 (d). Every failure gets one response whether or not the user exists. Failed sign-ins back off per account, doubling from 1 s up to 15 min (`failed_logins`, `next_login_at`); admins are slowed, never hard-locked; success resets both. `POST /auth/logout` revokes the presenting session. | (1) Unknown username, disabled user, wrong password, missing or wrong admin code, reused code, and an attempt during back-off all get the same status (401) and byte-identical bodies. (2) The unknown-user and back-off paths run one Argon2id verification against a fixed dummy hash (unit test with a counting hasher), so they take about as long as a wrong password. (3) Fake clock: after n consecutive failures, `next_login_at` = time of the failure + min(2^(n−1), 900) s, giving 1, 2, 4, …, 512, 900, 900 s; the correct password before `next_login_at` is refused; after it, sign-in succeeds and sets `failed_logins` = 0 and `next_login_at` = NULL (counting of attempts made during back-off: #55). (4) After 100 failures an admin can still sign in 15 min after the last failure (no hard lock), and `failed_logins` does not overflow its SMALLINT. (5) The new session row has `kind = 'session'`, `expires_at` = `created_at` + 24 h and `totp_verified` as stated. (6) After logout the token is refused; logout with a `bsk_` token is refused and does not revoke it. (7) The repeated-failure alert follows #54; audit entries follow #50. (8) The response body has the #47 fields and never contains `password_hash`, `totp_secret_enc` or a token hash. | manager |
| REQ-049 | TOTP enrolment and replacement | TS §6.2 (L1085, L1087, L1089, L1096), TS §8.4 (L1290), TS §8.1 (L1242); #47, #49, #50 | `POST /auth/totp/enrol` (session only, any signed-in user) enrols TOTP for the caller; replacing an existing seed needs the current password and a current code. Enrolment completes only when the caller proves a code from the new seed (protocol: #49). The caller's session is then re-issued as TOTP-verified: a new `bsu_` token is returned and the old one is revoked. "TOTP enrolled" and "TOTP replaced" each alert and are audit-logged. | (1) A `bsk_` token is refused. (2) An admin without TOTP enrols: the response holds a new token with `totp_verified = true`; the old token is refused afterwards; the new one can call `/users`. (3) Replacement without the password, with a wrong password, without a code, or with a wrong or reused code is refused and the stored seed is unchanged. (4) A wrong confirmation code leaves any existing seed unchanged and still valid. (5) One alert entry (`detail.alert = true`, kind per #50) is written for an enrolment and one for a replacement. (6) The seed is stored only as vault ciphertext (REQ-043). (7) Viewers and operators can enrol too. | manager |
| REQ-050 | Change own password | TS §6.2 (L1086, L1087, L1097); #53 | `POST /auth/password` (session only, any signed-in user) needs the current password; the new password must meet REQ-041. A password change ends the user's sessions (whether the presenting session ends too: #53). It is audit-logged. | (1) A `bsk_` token is refused. (2) A wrong current password is refused and nothing changes. (3) An 11-character new password is refused. (4) On success the new password verifies and the old one does not; every other session of the user is refused afterwards; the presenting session follows #53; the user's API tokens still work. (5) One audit entry is written (#50), with no password in it. | manager |
| REQ-051 | API tokens: create, list, revoke | TS §6.2 (L1081, L1085, L1091, L1098–L1099), TS §2.3 (L123), TS §8.4 (L1290), TS §8.1 (L1242); #8, #47, #52 | `POST /api-tokens` creates a `bsk_` token for the caller: session only, a TOTP session for admins, minimum role viewer, lifetime 90 days by default and at most 365, an optional name of at most 64 characters; the token is shown once, and creation alerts. `GET /api-tokens` lists the caller's own API tokens without the token or its hash. `DELETE /api-tokens` revokes the caller's own (shape: #47). | (1) With defaults, `expires_at` = creation + 90 days; a lifetime of 365 days is accepted; 366, 0 and negative values are refused. (2) The create response holds the token once; `GET` never includes the token or `token_hash`. (3) A user cannot list or revoke another user's tokens, including by guessing an id (refused or not found, per #48). (4) A revoked token is refused on its next request. (5) Each creation writes one alert entry (`detail.alert = true`). (6) `DELETE` called with a `bsk_` token follows #8. (7) An API token row always has `totp_verified = false`. (8) A `bsk_` token cannot create another (REQ-046 (2)). | manager |
| REQ-052 | User accounts and roles | TS §6.2 (L1085, L1086, L1089–L1091, L1125), TS §5.3 (L1050), TS §8.4 (L1290), TS §8.1 (L1253); #7, #47, #50, #53, #56 | CRUD `/users` (admin, TOTP session): create (default role `viewer`, initial password per #7), list, read, change role, disable (`DELETE` means disable), reset another user's TOTP, and revoke a user's tokens. Users are never deleted. Promoting a user to admin requires TOTP already enrolled. A role change or a TOTP reset ends the user's sessions. Disabling, demoting or revoking a user's tokens revokes every API token they own; disabling revokes their sessions too. The last active admin cannot be disabled or demoted. Every change is audit-logged; user created, role granted, admin created and TOTP reset alert. | (1) Create gives an `app_user` row with an Argon2id hash and the requested role; a duplicate username is refused (409) with no change; username rules per #56. (2) `DELETE /users/{id}` sets `disabled = true`, keeps the row and revokes all of the user's tokens. (3) Promoting a user without TOTP to admin is refused (409 or 422, #48); with TOTP it succeeds and alerts. (4) Any role change revokes the user's sessions; a demotion also revokes their API tokens. (5) The last active admin cannot be disabled or demoted. PostgreSQL: with two active admins demoting each other at the same time on two connections, at most one succeeds and at least one active admin remains. (6) A TOTP reset by another admin clears `totp_secret_enc` and `totp_last_step`, ends the user's sessions and alerts; that admin's next sign-in gets the restricted session. (7) Revoking a user's tokens revokes their API tokens (sessions per #53). (8) Each change writes its audit entry in the same transaction; a forced rollback leaves neither the change nor the entry. (9) No response contains `password_hash`, `totp_secret_enc` or a token hash. | manager |
| REQ-053 | Every audit entry streamed to the external sink | TS §5.3 (L1051), TS §8.6 (L1315), TS §8.1 (L1240, L1256), TS §8.4 (L1289); #24, #51 | Every committed `audit_log` entry (written by the API, by the CLI and later by the retention task; delivery model #51) is sent to `BITSCAN_AUDIT_SINK` in `audit_id` order, with every column including `prev_hash` and `row_hash`, so the external store can verify the chain. Wire format and transport security: #24. An entry whose transaction rolls back is never sent. | (1) Against a fake sink for each #24 transport, every committed entry arrives in `audit_id` order; after a crash and restart, a resend carries the same `audit_id` and `row_hash`, so duplicates are detectable. (2) An entry written in a rolled-back transaction never arrives. (3) The delivered fields re-hash with `compute_row_hash` to the delivered `row_hash`. (4) The sink connection verifies the sink's TLS certificate; a sink URL with a plain transport (`udp`, unencrypted `tcp`, `http://`) is refused at start-up (#24). (5) With the sink up, an entry arrives within the delivery bound of #51 after commit. (6) Entries written by the CLI while `serve` runs arrive too (#51). | manager |
| REQ-054 | Disk queue, outage alert and the 15-minute refusal | TS §5.3 (L1051), TS §8.1 (L1256), TS §6.2 (L1084), TS §8.7 (L1344, L1377); #1, #48, #51 | While the sink is unreachable, entries are queued on disk in `/var/lib/bitscan/audit-queue` (the path is a parameter so tests can use a temporary folder), an "audit sink unreachable" alert fires at once, and once the outage has lasted 15 minutes every TOTP-session action is refused until the queue has drained. | (1) Fake sink down: new entries are written to the queue folder, survive a restart of the shipper, and are sent in `audit_id` order and removed from the queue once the sink is back. Queue files are readable only by the manager's user (folder 0700, files 0600). (2) Exactly one "audit sink unreachable" alert entry is written per outage, at the first failed delivery. (3) Fake clock: at 14 min 59 s of outage `/users` still works; at 15 min it is refused (503 class, #48) and changes nothing; after the sink is back and the queue is empty it works again. (4) Routes that are not TOTP-session routes (sign-in, `/api-tokens`) keep working during the outage. (5) Restarting the manager during an outage does not restart the 15-minute clock (#51). (6) A queue record cut off by a simulated crash mid-write is detected; earlier records are still sent and the cut-off record is never sent as a corrupt entry. | manager |
| REQ-055 | Alert delivery | TS §8.4 (L1289–L1291); #5, #50, #54 | Every alert is an audit entry with `detail.alert = true`, so it reaches the sink, and increments `bitscan_alerts_total{kind}`. M2 raises: API token created; TOTP enrolled or replaced; TOTP reset; user created, role granted or admin created; audit sink unreachable; repeated sign-in failures and repeated 401s (thresholds: #54). | (1) Each listed event writes exactly one audit entry with `detail.alert = true` and the `kind` of #50, in the same transaction as the change. (2) `bitscan_alerts_total{kind}` rises by 1 after the commit, and not at all when the transaction rolls back. (3) The counter lives in a registry that a later metrics route can serve; M2 exposes no metrics route (#5). | manager |
| REQ-056 | Health endpoints | TS §8.4 (L1292), TS §3.4 (L230), TS §8.3 Integration (L1278); #5, #41 | `/healthz` reports liveness. `/readyz` returns 200 only when the database is reachable as `bitscan_app`, the Alembic revision equals the head shipped in the package, and the `system_schedule` rows (`inventory`, `offline_sweep`, `retention`) are present. Prefix and authentication: #5 (default: outside `/api/v1`, no token). | (1) `/healthz` returns 200 while the database is unreachable. (2) `/readyz` returns 200 on a freshly migrated database. (3) `/readyz` returns 503 when the database is unreachable; when the stored revision is not the packaged head (changed as `bitscan_owner`); when the `inventory` row is missing (deleted as `bitscan_owner`, since `bitscan_app` cannot); and likewise for `offline_sweep` and `retention`. (4) A 503 body is problem details naming the failed check, with no DSN, host name or driver message. (5) Neither route writes to the database. (6) Both carry the REQ-036 headers. | manager |
| REQ-057 | `bitscan-manager serve` | TS §8.7 (L1343, L1345, L1359–L1371), TS §8.6 (L1302–L1315), TS §6 (L1056), TS §2.3 (L118), TS §2.1 (L103), TS §8.2 (L1269); #55 | `serve` loads the serve profile (REQ-019), builds the runtime (database pool, vault client, sink shipper, rate limiter) and serves `create_app()` in one process on `BITSCAN_LISTEN`, over HTTPS only with TLS 1.2 or later, using the certificate and key files. `SIGHUP` reloads the certificate without closing the listener. It never reads `BITSCAN_MIGRATE_DSN_FILE`. | (1) A missing or invalid serve variable makes `serve` exit non-zero before binding the port; stderr names each variable, and no canary appears. (2) TLS 1.2 and 1.3 handshakes succeed; TLS 1.0 and 1.1 handshakes and a plain-HTTP request fail. (3) After the certificate and key files are replaced and `SIGHUP` is sent, new connections get the new certificate while the process and listening socket stay the same; a `SIGHUP` with an unreadable or mismatched new pair keeps the old certificate and logs an error with no key material. (4) `/healthz` over TLS carries the REQ-036 headers. (5) `X-Forwarded-For` does not change the client address seen by the app (#55). (6) `SIGTERM` stops `serve` cleanly: the pending failed-check counts are written (REQ-047 (4)) and the audit queue is left intact. (7) With `BITSCAN_MIGRATE_DSN_FILE` pointing to an unreadable file, `serve` starts normally. (8) REQ-008 is narrowed again: `serve` is a real command. | manager |
| REQ-058 | Server CLI: `users create-admin`, `users create-api-token`, `users reset-totp` | TS §8.7 (L1347), TS §6.2 (L1081, L1089, L1090), TS §8.4 (L1290), TS §5.3 (L1049); #7, #47, #50, #51 | The `users` commands run as root and read the `bitscan_app` DSN from `/etc/bitscan/secrets/app.dsn`, with the TLS check of REQ-019 (5). `create-admin <name>` creates an admin without TOTP (initial password: #7). `create-api-token <user>` prints a new `bsk_` token for an existing, enabled user once (90 days by default; other options: #47). `reset-totp <user>` clears the user's TOTP and ends their sessions. Each writes its audit entry and alert. | (1) `create-admin` creates a row with role `admin`, no TOTP and an Argon2id hash; an existing username exits non-zero with no change; the first sign-in of that admin gets the restricted session. (2) The password is never read from the command line or the environment, and must meet REQ-041 (#7). (3) `create-api-token`: stdout holds the token and nothing else secret; the database holds only its hash; an unknown or disabled user exits non-zero with no change. (4) `reset-totp` clears `totp_secret_enc` and `totp_last_step`, revokes the user's sessions and alerts. (5) A missing, unreadable or non-TLS DSN file exits non-zero with a message naming the file path and no canary. (6) The commands open no network connection other than PostgreSQL. (7) Each command writes its audit entry and alert (actor per #50); while `serve` runs they reach the sink (#51). (8) Unknown `users` subcommands exit non-zero and touch no database. | manager |
| REQ-059 | Web client regenerated, `baseUrl` rechecked | TS §8.2 (L1266), TS §6 (L1056); decisions 2026-10-20 (TASK-006 row); #5, #6; PD-20 | Once the M2 routes are in `manager/openapi.json`, `web/src/api/schema.d.ts` is regenerated. Every user-route path in it starts with `/api/v1/`, so `client.ts` keeps `baseUrl ""` (same origin; the serving method stays open under #6). | (1) G16 (OpenAPI client regeneration with no diff) is green at the M2 head. (2) Every M2 user-route path in the generated types starts with `/api/v1/`; health paths follow #5. (3) `client.ts` contains no absolute origin, and `npm run typecheck` passes with a type-level test that a call to the sign-in path compiles. | web |

### Changes to earlier requirements (M2)

- REQ-006 (1) and (2) are superseded for routes: from TASK-038 on, the app has user routes and, from TASK-044, health routes. REQ-006 (1) still holds for `/docs`, `/docs/oauth2-redirect`, `/redoc` and `/openapi.json` (REQ-035 (3)).
- REQ-007 (3) (`paths` is `{}`) is superseded from TASK-038 on; the export stays deterministic (REQ-007 (1), (2), (4), (5)).
- REQ-008 (2) is narrowed again: `serve` (TASK-045) and `users create-admin`, `users create-api-token`, `users reset-totp` (TASK-046) become real commands. `bits rebuild` and unknown commands keep the M0 behaviour.
- REQ-019: the serve profile gets its first consumer (TASK-045); its criteria are unchanged.

## M3 agent API and scheduler (inventory only)

Scope, from the lead's M3 request (2026-10-22) and the milestone reorder and carry-over rows (`work/decisions.md`, 2026-10-20; M2 rows 2026-10-21 and 2026-10-22):
- the six TS §6.1 agent routes on their own router and token check; the results route answers 409 for every post, because no compliance job exists before M9;
- the TS §6.5 wire contract, including the full compliance Job and Results schemas, frozen in `manager/openapi.json` now, with request and response fixtures shared with the M4 Go agent;
- enrolment tokens, enrolment, approval and rejection, the agent token check, token rotation;
- heartbeat, job pull (inventory jobs only), inventory upload and ingestion, inventory queueing and `offline_sweep`;
- user routes `/enrollment-tokens`, `/enrollment-requests` (list, approve, reject), `GET /agents`, `/agents/{id}`, `/agents/{id}/packages`, `PUT /agents/{id}/tags`, `POST /agents/{id}/revoke`, `GET` and `PATCH /settings`, `GET /audit-log` (moved from M2, decisions 2026-10-21) and the metrics route;
- the M3 alerts and metrics of TS §8.4, including the clock-skew alert (M2 carry-over);
- carry-overs: body caps on templated agent routes, the metrics route versus `HealthPath`, OpenAPI paths with the full `/api/v1` prefix and the web client regenerated at the end, OpenTelemetry rechecked after new app wiring.

Not in M3: package search and CSV, and the UI (M5); the Go agent and `.deb` (M4); importer, evaluator, scans, compliance job building, result intake and the runner (M7 to M11); retention (M11), including the 30-day inventory-job, 180-day snapshot, 90-day decided-request and unreferenced-package rules; `GET /agents/{id}/scans` (M9: it lists scans, none exist before M9, and its item shape is the `GET /scans` item); the load and end-to-end tests (M6); a CLI command that creates `bse_` tokens (`work/decisions.md` 2026-10-20, "Not to be added"); anything in the CLAUDE.md out-of-scope list.

**Migrations: none.** Every column M3 uses exists in TS §5.2: `agent` (L712–L731), `agent_ip_seen` (L734–L740), `agent_tag` (L742–L746), `enrollment_token` (L748–L759), `enrollment_request` (L761–L777), `os_info` (L780–L793), `package` (L795–L804), `agent_package` (L806–L815), `inventory_snapshot` (L817–L825), `system_schedule` (L827–L833), `setting` (L835–L840), `scan_job` (L872–L890), `audit_log` (L981–L990). The TS §5.2 grants (L994–L1013) already give `bitscan_app` what M3 needs: UPDATE on `agent`, `enrollment_token`, `enrollment_request`, `os_info`, `agent_package`, `agent_ip_seen`, `setting`, `system_schedule`; DELETE on `agent_tag`, `agent_package`, `agent_ip_seen`; the `scan_job` progress columns; INSERT only on `package` and `inventory_snapshot`. An answer that needs a new column (for example storing the heartbeat fields of #25) is a schema change for the owner.

Conventions for this section, in addition to the M2 ones. An "agent route" is any route under `/api/v1/agent/`. "Refused" on an agent route means the status and problem `type` fixed by #59; on a user route, by #48 as in M2. "As owner" means a test-only statement run as `bitscan_owner` to build a state the app cannot (for example a compliance `scan_job` row). The canary sweep also plants `bsa_` and `bse_` tokens, inventory package names and results stdout and stderr. "Alert entry" means one `audit_log` row with `detail.alert = true` and the #71 kind.

| REQ-060 | Agent wire contract in the OpenAPI document | TS §6.5 (L1159–L1190), §6.1 (L1060–L1067), §3.5 (L248, L253), §8.3 Contract (L1277); #59, #60 | Pydantic models define every request and response body of the six agent routes exactly as TS §6.5, with the field constraints of #60: Enrol, Heartbeat, Job pull (`{"jobs": [Job]}`, where Job is either the inventory form or the full compliance form with `plugins` and `redaction_patterns`), Results request and response, Inventory body, Rotate. By the M3 head every model is referenced by its route in `manager/openapi.json`, so M9 and M10 need no contract change. | (1) The exported `manager/openapi.json` has each of the six agent operations under `/api/v1/agent/…` with its request and response schema. The Job schema has an inventory variant with exactly `job_id`, `agent_id`, `kind` (`"inventory"`) and `pickup_by`, and a compliance variant with exactly those (`kind` `"compliance"`) plus `plugins` (items exactly `plugin_uuid`, `execute`, `timeout_s`) and `redaction_patterns`; both have `additionalProperties: false`, and no schema reachable from the job-pull response has an `operator`, `expected_result`, `eval_spec` or `expected` property. (2) The heartbeat response schema has exactly `job_pull_seconds`, `max_parallel_plugins` and `server_time`. (4) Every request schema refuses an extra key, a missing required key and a wrong type; timestamps need a UTC offset; `machine_id`, UUIDs and `list_sha256` have the #60 formats. (5) Every agent operation documents its refusals as `application/problem+json` with the #59 statuses, and no operation lists 422 for request validation (M2 contract). (6) Re-exporting gives a byte-identical file (G6). | manager |

| REQ-063 | Agent and enrolment token format and storage | TS §6.2 (L1083), §3.2 (L183), §2.3 (L124–L125), §5.2 (L717, L721, L750, L770); security invariants "Secrets and output"; #56 (a) | `bsa_` and `bse_` tokens are the prefix plus the unpadded base64url of 32 CSPRNG bytes (43 characters, #56 (a)). Only the lowercase SHA-256 hex of the whole token is stored (`agent.token_hash`, `agent.prev_token_hash`, `enrollment_token.token_hash`, `enrollment_request.new_token_hash`). Each token is returned exactly once. | (1) 10,000 generated tokens of each kind are distinct, carry their prefix, are 47 characters long and decode to 32 bytes. (2) Each stored hash equals the SHA-256 hex digest of the token string. (3) After 20 enrolments, 5 rotations and 5 enrolment tokens, no text, char or JSON column of any table (`audit_log.detail` included) contains any issued token. (4) The user-token parser still refuses `bsa_` and `bse_` (REQ-040 (4)); the agent parser refuses `bsu_`, `bsk_`, wrong lengths and characters outside base64url. (5) No route other than the creating one returns a token or its hash. | manager |
| REQ-064 | Agent token check | TS §3.2 (L185–L191), §6.1 (L1069–L1070), §8.4 (L1290), §5.2 (L712–L777); #25, #26, #59, #62, #63 | Every `bsa_` route resolves the token against `agent.token_hash`, `agent.prev_token_hash` (rotation overlap, #62) and `enrollment_request.new_token_hash` (held re-enrolment), in the order of #59, and answers: unknown → 401; revoked → 401 `token_revoked`; expired → 401 `token_expired`; a valid token whose `X-Bitscan-Machine-Id` differs from the stored machine-id (a missing header: #63) → 401 `machine_id_mismatch`, the token is revoked and an alert is raised; held (agent `awaiting_approval`, or a pending re-enrolment) → 403 `pending_approval`; rejected enrolment → 401 `enrollment_rejected` (#26); an `{id}` that is not the token's agent → 403 (#59). An accepted call records the source IP (#25). | (1) PostgreSQL, on each of the five routes: each case gives exactly its status and `type`. (2) After a mismatch, the same token with the right header gets 401 `token_revoked`, `token_revoked_at` is set, and exactly one `machine_id_mismatch` alert entry exists. (3) A held re-enrolment's new token gets 403 `pending_approval` while the agent's old token still works; after rejection the new token gets 401 `enrollment_rejected` and the old row and token behave as #26 says. (4) A rejected new enrolment's token gets 401 `enrollment_rejected`. (5) Fake clock: a token is accepted one second before `token_expires_at` and refused at it with `token_expired`. (6) Agent A's valid token on agent B's path gets 403 and changes nothing for B. (7) A decommissioned agent's token gets 401 `token_revoked`. (8) A 403 `pending_approval` is not counted as a failed check (#59). | manager |
| REQ-065 | Agent rate limits and failed token checks | TS §6.3 (L1136), §8.1 (L1248, L1257), §8.4 (L1291); #54 (b), #55 (c), (d), #59, #65 | Agent routes: 30 requests per minute per agent. `POST /agent/enroll`: 10 per minute per source IP, every attempt counting (as #55 (c)). Failed token checks on agent routes feed the existing 20-per-minute per-IP limit and its aggregated audit entry; valid tokens are never throttled by it. Over a limit: 429 `rate_limited` with `Retry-After`. Repeated 401s from one agent alert (#65). | (1) Fake clock: the 31st request of one agent within a minute, over any mix of its routes, gets 429 `rate_limited` with `Retry-After`; another agent is not affected; the next minute it is accepted. (2) The 11th enrol attempt from one IP within a minute gets 429; another IP is not affected. (3) After 20 bad agent tokens from one IP in a minute the 21st gets 429, while a valid `bsa_` from that IP still succeeds; the minute's single aggregated entry counts all 21, together with any user-route failures from that IP. (4) The per-agent 401 alert follows #65. | manager |
| REQ-066 | Body caps on agent routes | TS §6.3 (L1138), §3.4 (L240), §6.1 (L1072), §8.1 (L1250); #57 (b); M2 gate review carry-over | The body cap registry matches path templates, so `/api/v1/agent/{id}/inventory` gets 10 MiB as sent and `/api/v1/agent/{id}/jobs/{job_id}/results` 1.5 MiB as sent and after decompression; every other agent route keeps 1 MiB. On the inventory and results routes the cap is enforced after the token check, so a refusal can be audit-logged and (inventory) fail its job; the app still never reads more than cap + 1 bytes. | (1) On real agent paths (random UUIDs), an inventory body of exactly 10 MiB passes the cap and 10 MiB + 1 byte gets 413 `too_large`; the same at 1.5 MiB on results; heartbeat refuses 1 MiB + 1. (2) With a `Content-Length` over the cap, or a chunked body over it, at most cap + 1 bytes are read. (3) On inventory and results, an over-cap body with an invalid token gets 401 (token checked first), and with a valid token gets 413 and one audit entry. (4) A gzip results body that decompresses to more than 1.5 MiB gets 413 after at most 1.5 MiB + 1 decompressed bytes. (5) A cap above its class maximum, or two caps for one template, still raise at app creation (REQ-037 (5)). (6) User routes keep the 1 MiB cap. | manager |
| REQ-067 | Enrolment tokens | TS §3.2 (L181), §2.3 (L124), §6.2 (L1084, L1116), §8.4 (L1290), §5.2 (L748–L759); #68, #71 | CRUD `/api/v1/enrollment-tokens` (admin, TOTP session, refused during the 15-minute sink outage): create with `max_uses` 1–10,000 (default 1), an expiry (default 24 h, range #68), optional `allowed_cidrs` (none = any source) and `auto_approve` (default false); the `bse_` token is shown once. List and read never show the token or its hash. Delete revokes (`revoked_at`); no row is deleted. Creating a token with `auto_approve` or more than 100 uses alerts. Every create and revoke is audit-logged. | (1) Defaults give `max_uses` 1, `expires_at` = creation + 24 h, `allowed_cidrs` NULL and `auto_approve` false. (2) `max_uses` 0 or 10,001, an invalid CIDR and an expiry outside the #68 range are refused (400) and write no row. (3) The create response holds the token once; list and read never contain it or `token_hash`. (4) `auto_approve: true`, or `max_uses: 101`, writes exactly one alert entry; `max_uses: 100` without auto-approve writes none. (5) An admin `bsk_` token, an unverified admin session and operator or viewer sessions are refused on every method; while the sink gate refuses, every method is refused. (6) After delete, enrolling with the token is refused (#59) and the row still exists with `revoked_at` set. | manager |
| REQ-068 | Enrolment (`POST /agent/enroll`) | TS §3.2 (L182–L188), §6.5 (L1161), §8.1 (L1248), §8.4 (L1291), §5.2 (L712–L777); #55 (d), #59, #60, #61, #64, #71 | The manager checks the `bse_` token: not expired or revoked, the source IP (the TCP peer, #55 (d)) inside `allowed_cidrs` when set, and `uses < max_uses` with an atomic increment. A new machine-id without it: agent row `awaiting_approval` holding the token hash, plus an `enrollment_request` with `kind = 'new'`, `status: "awaiting_approval"`. An existing machine-id, whatever the token allows: the existing `agent_id` is returned, the new hash is kept only in a `kind = 'reenrol'` request, `status: "awaiting_approval"` (#64). More than `enrolment_alert_per_hour` enrolments on one token alert (#61). | (1) Each refusal case (expired, revoked, source outside the CIDRs, exhausted, unknown) answers per #59, changes no row and does not increment `uses`. (2) PostgreSQL: with `max_uses` 5 and 20 concurrent enrolments from 20 machine-ids, exactly 5 succeed and `uses` ends at 5. (3) Each of the three outcomes writes exactly the rows stated, with `token_expires_at` = `issued_at` + `agent_token_ttl_days`, and the response validates against the Enrol schema. (4) A re-enrolment with an `auto_approve` token is still held, and the existing agent row (token hash, status, tags, `os_info`) is unchanged. (5) An IPv6 source and a CIDR list mixing IPv4 and IPv6 entries are checked correctly. | REQ-069 | Enrolment requests: list, approve, reject | TS §3.2 (L185–L188, L192), §6.2 (L1084, L1114–L1115), §8.4 (L1290), §5.2 (L761–L777); #26, #64, #68, #71 | `GET /api/v1/enrollment-requests` (admin, session or API token) lists pending and decided requests with hostname, source IP, OS and every other agent that has the same hostname or machine-id. Approve and reject (admin, TOTP session, sink gate) decide a pending request once. Approving a new enrolment moves the agent from `awaiting_approval` to `enrolled`. Approving a re-enrolment re-links the existing agent row (id and history kept) to the new token, which revokes the old token, and alerts. Rejecting keeps the request with its decision and sets a new agent row to `decommissioned` (never deleted); a rejected re-enrolment follows #26. Every decision is audit-logged. | (1) The list shows each request's hostname, `source_ip`, `os_pretty_name` and the matching agents; a cloned image (two hosts, one machine-id) and two hosts sharing a hostname are each listed with their match. (2) After approving a new request, the agent's token passes the check and its status is `enrolled`. (3) After approving a re-enrolment, the old token gets 401, the new one works, and `agent_id`, tags, `os_info` and packages are unchanged; exactly one `reenrolment_approved` alert entry exists. (4) After rejecting a new request, the agent row still exists with status `decommissioned`. (5) Deciding an already decided request is refused (409) and changes nothing; two concurrent decisions on one request give exactly one success. (6) An admin `bsk_` token, an unverified admin session, an operator session, and any session while the sink gate refuses, are refused on approve and reject; `GET` works with an admin `bsk_` token and is refused to an operator. | manager |
| REQ-070 | Heartbeat | TS §3.3 (L202, L206, L211–L212), §3.7 (L484), §6.5 (L1163), §8.4 (L1290–L1291); #25, #65, #71, #73 | `POST /agent/{id}/heartbeat` validates the TS §6.5 body, records `last_heartbeat_at`, `last_seen_ip`, `agent_version` and `runner_helpers`, and answers exactly `{job_pull_seconds, max_parallel_plugins, server_time}` from the current settings. A change of `runner_helpers` is audit-logged and alerts. A `clock` more than 5 minutes from `server_time` alerts (repeats #65); the entry holds both times, so a manager-wide skew (every host off by the same amount, as in the `db maintain` residual) can be told apart from one host's. The heartbeat fields with no column follow #25. A heartbeat never queues, returns or starts a job. | (1) The response has exactly the three keys; after `PATCH /settings` changes `job_pull_seconds`, the next heartbeat returns the new value; `server_time` is the fake clock's time in RFC 3339 UTC. (2) `last_heartbeat_at` and `last_seen_ip` are set, and the status follows #73. (3) `runner_helpers` changing from `[]` to `["capread"]` writes one audit entry and one `runner_helper_changed` alert entry; sending the same list again writes nothing. (4) A `clock` 5 min 1 s from the server time alerts; exactly 5 min does not; repeats follow #65. With the manager's fake clock 150 days ahead of three agents, each of their first heartbeats alerts. (5) The heartbeat creates no `scan_job` row and changes no job. (6) `last_error` is handled as #25 says and is never written to an audit entry. | manager |
| REQ-071 | Agent token rotation | TS §3.2 (L190–L191), §6.5 (L1188), §8.4 (L1290), §5.2 (L719–L723, L734–L740); #62 | `POST /agent/{id}/token/rotate` issues a new token (lifetime from the current `agent_token_ttl_days`) only inside the window from `issued_at + max(TTL − 30 d, TTL ÷ 2)` to `expires_at`, where TTL is the presented token's own `expires_at − issued_at`, and at most once per 24 h, counting only rotations whose new token was then used. The old token stays valid as #62 states. A retry with the old token before the new one is used returns a fresh token and the unused one stops working. Old-token use after the new token was used follows #62 (both revoked, alert). A rotation outside the window is refused (#62) and alerts; one from an IP not in `agent_ip_seen` for that agent in the last 7 days alerts. After `expires_at` every call, rotate included, gets 401 `token_expired`. | (1) Fake clock, TTL 365 days: rotate on day 334 is refused and alerts; on day 335 it succeeds, and the response validates against the Rotate schema with `expires_at` = now + the current TTL. (2) TTL 60 days: the window opens on day 30. (3) Lost response: a second rotate with the old token returns another new token; the first new token then gets 401; the old token still works. (4) Overlap and reuse follow #62, checked with fake-clock steps at 9 min 59 s and 10 min after the new token's first use. (5) A second rotation within 24 h after a used one is refused (#62); after an unused one it is allowed. (6) A rotation from a never-seen IP alerts once; from an IP last seen 6 days ago it does not; 8 days ago it does. (7) After `expires_at`, rotate gets 401 `token_expired`. (8) Changing `agent_token_ttl_days` changes only tokens issued or rotated afterwards. | manager |
| REQ-072 | Job pull | TS §3.3 (L203, L210–L214), §6.1 (L1064), §6.5 (L1165–L1182), §4.4 (L566–L567, L572), §3.5 (L248); security invariants "Never add", "Secrets and output" | `GET /agent/{id}/jobs` returns `{"jobs": […]}` with at most 10 of that agent's `queued` jobs whose `pickup_by` is still in the future, oldest first, and marks them `dispatched` with `dispatched_at`. Each job body is the stored `payload` (TS §6.5); an inventory job has exactly `job_id`, `agent_id`, `kind` and `pickup_by`. In M3 only inventory jobs are returned. `eval_spec` is never sent. | (1) With 12 queued jobs, a pull returns 10, oldest first, and the next pull returns 2; each job is returned and dispatched exactly once. (2) PostgreSQL: two concurrent pulls by one agent never return the same job. (3) Other agents' jobs, jobs in any state other than `queued`, and jobs past `pickup_by` are never returned. (4) A compliance job row inserted as owner (with an `eval_spec`) is not returned in M3, and no pull response contains an `eval_spec`, `operator` or `expected` key. (5) An empty queue returns `{"jobs": []}` and changes nothing. (6) Every response validates against the job-pull schema. | manager |
| REQ-073 | Results route in M3 | TS §6.1 (L1065, L1071), §6.5 (L1184, L1190), §3.5 (L253), §8.4 (L1291); #54 (c), #65, #71 | `POST /agent/{id}/jobs/{job_id}/results` exists with the frozen Results contract, its caps and gzip handling. No compliance job exists in M3, so every well-formed post answers 409 `job_not_acceptable`, stores nothing and is audit-logged. More than 20 such 409s from one agent within 10 minutes alert (#54 (c); repeats #65). | (1) A valid batch for one of the agent's inventory jobs, for an unknown job and for another agent's job, and an empty start notice, each get 409 `job_not_acceptable`; no `scan_result` row is written and no job changes state. (2) Each 409 writes one audit entry (#71) holding no stdout or stderr. (3) A malformed body gets 400 and an over-cap body 413 (REQ-066). (4) 21 refused posts within 10 minutes from one agent write one alert entry; 20 write none. (5) No stdout or stderr from a posted batch appears in any log record. | manager |
| REQ-074 | Inventory decoder | TS §3.4 (L240), §6.5 (L1186), §6.1 (L1072), §8.1 (L1250), §8.3 Property/fuzz (L1276); #33, #60, #72 | The decoder takes the uploaded body as a stream, gunzips it with a hard 64 MiB cap, parses the TS §6.5 inventory body and checks: at most 50,000 packages, every field against its column (#60), `os_info.raw` at most 256 KiB, and `list_sha256` against the value computed from `packages` (#33). It returns either a complete decoded inventory or one refusal (status per #72); never partial data. | (1) A body that decompresses to 64 MiB + 1 byte is refused (413) after at most 64 MiB + 1 decompressed bytes; a gzip bomb (about 1 MiB expanding to 1 GiB) is refused within that bound, with peak memory under 256 MiB. (2) 50,001 packages, each over-length field, a `raw` of 256 KiB + 1 byte, duplicate packages and a `list_sha256` mismatch are each refused with the #72 status. (3) The decoder's `list_sha256` for a fixture equals a value the test computes independently from TS §6.5 L1186 and the #33 sort order. (4) Property and fuzz (G32): random and truncated gzip, random JSON, deep nesting and invalid UTF-8 never raise anything other than the decoder's refusal, and never return partial data. (5) The valid inventory fixtures of TP-M3-01 decode to their expected values. | manager |
| REQ-075 | Inventory ingestion | TS §3.4 (L241–L243), §5.2 (L780–L825), §7.4 (L1229), §8.1 (L1257); database rules ("Never weaken these grants"); #65, #69 | For each accepted upload, in one transaction: replace `os_info` (`collected_at` per #69) and set `agent.last_inventory_at`. If `list_sha256` differs from the agent's last snapshot (or there is none): insert unknown `package` rows with `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and then select them, upsert `agent_package` (keeping `first_seen_at`, moving `last_seen_at`), delete the agent's rows for packages no longer listed, and write an `inventory_snapshot` with `added` and `removed` (#69). One agent adding more than 1,000 previously unseen packages in a day alerts (#65). | (1) First upload: one `os_info` row, N `agent_package` rows and one snapshot listing all N in `added`. (3) One package upgraded and another removed: one snapshot whose `added` and `removed` follow #69; the removed package's `agent_package` row is gone and its `package` row remains. ### Changes to earlier requirements (M3)

- REQ-035 (5) now covers the agent routes too: every agent path in `manager/openapi.json` starts with `/api/v1/agent/`.
- REQ-037: the cap registry matches path templates and supports caps enforced after the token check (REQ-066); criteria (1) to (5) still hold for user routes.
- REQ-044 (1) is re-run with real agent and enrolment-token rows (REQ-062 (3)).
- REQ-047 (3), (4): the failed-check limit and its aggregated entry are fed by agent routes too (REQ-065 (3)).
- REQ-055 (3) is superseded by REQ-086: the registry is now served.
- REQ-059 is superseded for M3 by REQ-089.

## M4 Go agent and pre-release `.deb`

Scope, from the milestone reorder and carry-over rows (`work/decisions.md`, 2026-10-20) and the lead's M4 request (2026-10-24, user rule 7: plan M4 now, against the frozen agent contract):
- `bitscan-agent`: `agent.yaml`, the state directory, TLS with optional pinning, enrolment, the heartbeat and job-pull loops with back-off, the job checks, token rotation, `os_info` and dpkg collection, the dpkg watcher (inotify), gzip inventory upload;
- the agent side of the frozen wire contract (TS §6.5, `manager/openapi.json`, `manager/tests/contract/fixtures/` with its `schema_gaps`), including every 2026-10-23 M3 ruling that binds the agent (hyphenated lowercase UUIDs, NUL refused, nullable keys always present, 64-bit counters at most 2^53 − 1, `raw` nesting under the decoder's 32-container limit, gzip required on inventory, rotation window, the 24 h rule, the lost-response retry, the 30-day cap on an unused rotation, no concurrent rotation);
- the full package layout of a **pre-release 0.x** `.deb` for amd64: the three binaries (runner and capread idle, #74), the five units and the D-Bus policy verbatim, debconf, maintainer scripts, `lintian`, reproducible builds, SBOM, published SHA-256 (decisions 2026-10-20);
- the first Go dependency (so `go mod verify` can go red) (decisions 2026-10-20 carry-over);
- **amd64 only** (decisions 2026-10-24, "arm64 dropped from v1", an owner deviation from TS §3.1 and §8.3): nothing in M4 builds, packages, tests or emulates arm64, and the existing arm64 build and stub steps in `.github/workflows/agent.yml` are removed; the 2026-10-20 carry-over "arm64 stub execution" is withdrawn;
- manager carry-overs from M3: the `services/inventory/upload.py` Protocol seam (decisions 2026-10-23) and two README corrections (decisions 2026-10-23, "All M3 tasks approved").

Not in M4: the runner, plugin execution, redaction, the result spool and capread (M10); the 3-VM first-release gate, load test and supported release (M6); the manager install method (#6, M5); any manager behaviour change; anything in the CLAUDE.md out-of-scope list (no signed APT repository, no client certificates, no signing). The 0.x package is not supported as N−1 by later managers (decisions 2026-10-20).

Conventions for this section. L… are lines of `docs/spec/Technical_Specification.md`. "Fake manager" is a Go `httptest` TLS server in a `_test.go` file (the lint allows listeners only there) serving the frozen contract; "real manager" is the M3 `serve` in WSL. "Refused at start-up" means the agent exits non-zero before any network call with one line naming the key or file, and no secret in it. "Stops its loops" means the process stays running, makes no further manager call and logs one line saying the host must be re-enrolled (#81). Every agent requirement is tested on Linux (WSL Ubuntu 24.04 locally; CI per PD-27); none runs on Windows.

(2) Every request body the agent builds validates against its component schema in `manager/openapi.json`. (3) A response with an extra key, a missing nullable key, `pickup_by` with microseconds (`…T12:00:00.000000Z`) and an uppercase UUID: refused, refused, accepted, accepted (lowercase on output is the manager's, either case is accepted on input). (3) An accepted inventory job leads to one upload with `?job_id=` (REQ-101). (4) A compliance job is handled exactly as #74 says, and no command in it is executed by the agent. (3) A crash after the response and before the state write leaves the old token, which the next attempt rotates again.
