### Changes to earlier requirements (M4)

- REQ-010 (M0 stub binaries): `bitscan-agent` is no longer a stub; the stub-execution check keeps `bitscan-runner` and `bitscan-capread` only (#74, REQ-104, REQ-111). The arm64 half of REQ-010 (arm64 builds) is withdrawn: v1 is amd64 only (decisions 2026-10-24).
- REQ-016: the verbatim check also covers the D-Bus policy (REQ-106 (2)).
- REQ-061 (3): the Go half of "both pass the same fixtures" is REQ-091 (1).
- REQ-076: unchanged behaviour; its service gains the Protocol seam (REQ-112).

## M5 minimal host UI and package search

Planned 2026-10-24 (planner); consistency pass done. Awaiting the lead's review and the user's approval; open items #83–#89, #6 (M5 half), #32, PD-28–PD-30 (`work/open-questions.md`).

Scope, from the milestone reorder rows (`work/decisions.md` 2026-10-20: "First UI is the minimal host UI: login and TOTP, enrolment approval, host list and detail with `os_info` and packages, package search and CSV, added/removed-since-last-snapshot view"; "the bundler and UI serving decision (question #6) and the built-key lint nit move to M5") and the lead's M5 request (2026-10-24, planned alongside M4 under speed rule 7):
- manager: `GET /api/v1/packages/search` and `/packages/search.csv` (TS §6.2 L1117, §7.1, §7.2, §6.3 L1142), the only TS §6.2 routes M5 needs that M3 did not build; serving the built web UI from the manager's own origin (#6, M5 half); the `AgentItem.status` enum carry-over (decisions 2026-10-23, "TASK-075 revision 2 second approval given");
- web: the bundler and test environment; in-memory session handling; sign-in, TOTP enrolment and replacement, password change (#7 answer: "the UI offers a change"); enrolment tokens (create with a TOTP session, TS §3.2 L181, needed by the M6 gate) and the enrolment approval screen; host list and host detail with `os_info`, packages and "added / removed since last snapshot" (TS §7.3 L1225, #69); package search with type-ahead and CSV export (TS §7.3);
- CI: the built bundle checked for inline script and style (CSP `default-src 'self'`, TS §6.3 L1140);
- the README user guide for the UI.

What M3 already provides and M5 reuses unchanged: every auth route (M2), `/enrollment-tokens`, `/enrollment-requests` with `matches`, `GET /agents`, `/agents/{id}` with `os_info` and `last_snapshot` (#69), `/agents/{id}/packages` (#68), the problem-details and pagination machinery (#47, #48), the security-header middleware, and the generated client `web/src/api/client.ts` with its `UserPathsArePrefixed` guard.

Not in M5: UI screens for API tokens, users, settings, the audit log, host tags and agent revoke (PD-30; their routes exist and the CLI covers API tokens); anything compliance (policies, imports, scans, results: M7 to M11); `GET /agents/{id}/scans` (M9); the manager install method and packaging of the UI into the manager (#6, M6 half); the 10,000-agent heartbeat load test and every other long-running test (user, 2026-10-24: deferred until after M6), including any timing of the TS §7 "< 1 s at 10,000 × 2,000" search target (no TS §8.3 gate, testing.md open question); a policy editor and anything in the CLAUDE.md out-of-scope list.

**Migrations: none.** Search reads `package` (L795–L802), `agent_package` (L806–L814), `agent` (L712–L730), `agent_tag` (L742–L746) and `os_info` (L780–L793), with the existing indexes `ix_package_name_trgm` (L803), `ix_package_name_debver` (L804), `ix_agent_package_pkg` (L815) and `ix_agent_hostname_trgm` (L732), and only the SELECT grants `bitscan_app` already has. No index may be added.

Conventions for this section. L… are lines of `docs/spec/Technical_Specification.md`. "Refused" on a user route means 400 `invalid_request` (#48) unless stated. "UI test" means a Vitest component test in jsdom with Testing Library (PD-28), with the generated client's HTTP calls answered by a test double; it never needs a running manager. "Fixture fleet" is a PostgreSQL data set the tester builds through the M3 inventory ingestion (or as `bitscan_app` where the ingestion cannot express a case), with every expected answer computed independently in the test. "Hostile text" is a value such as `<script>alert(1)</script>`, `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` or `=HYPERLINK("http://x")`. Every UI requirement also holds REQ-132.

| REQ | Title | Spec ref | Statement | Acceptance criteria | Area |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| REQ-113 | Package search parameters | TS §7.1 (L1196–L1212), §6.3 (L1133–L1134); #48, #83 | `GET /api/v1/packages/search` takes exactly the TS §7.1 parameters `q`, `name`, `version`, `version_op` (`lt`, `le`, `eq`, `ge`, `gt`), `arch`, `status` (`installed` default, `any`), `os`, `tag`, `host`, `agent_id`, `group_by` (`package`, `host`, or absent), plus `limit` (1–500, default 50) and `cursor`. It refuses, with 400 `invalid_request`: `q` shorter than 3 characters, `q` together with `name`, `version_op` without `version`, a value outside an enum, an `os` value not in the #83 (c) form, an `agent_id` that is not a hyphenated UUID, a value over its #83 (h) length, an empty value or one holding U+0000 (#88), a `version` that is not a Debian version (#83 (g)), and a cursor from another query or whose anchor no longer exists (#83 (b)). | (1) Unit, one case each: `q=ab` refused, `q=abc` accepted; `q` with `name` refused; `version_op=lt` without `version` refused; `version_op=ne`, `status=removed`, `group_by=name` refused; `os=ubuntu` and `os=ubuntu:24.04` follow #83 (c); `agent_id=not-a-uuid` refused; each parameter one character over its #83 (h) limit refused and at the limit accepted; `name=` (empty) and `q=abc%00` refused (#88); every version in the M1 `dpkg` corpus accepted. (2) Through the API, every refusal is a problem+json 400 whose `detail` names the parameter and never echoes the value (REQ-038 (2)). (3) `limit=501` and `limit=0` are refused; omitted gives 50. (4) A cursor taken from a page with other filters or another `group_by` is refused (#83 (b)); a tampered cursor is refused (M2 machinery); a cursor whose last package or host has since been removed is refused. | manager |
| REQ-114 | Package search results | TS §7 (L1194), §7.1 (L1200–L1215), §7.2 (L1219), §5.2 (L795–L815); G3 (L15); #83 | Filters combine with AND. `q` matches names as #83 (a) says; `name` is an exact name; `version` alone is an exact version string; `version` with `version_op` compares in Debian order through `version::debversion` (never as text); `arch` is an exact architecture; `status=installed` keeps the #83 (f) statuses, `any` keeps every status; `os` matches the host's `os_info` (#83 (c)); `tag` is an exact host tag; `host` is a hostname substring (#83 (d)); `agent_id` is one host; which agents are searched is #83 (e). Without `group_by` each item is exactly `{name, version, architecture, status, agent_id, hostname}`; with `group_by=package` exactly `{name, version, architecture, host_count}`; with `group_by=host` exactly `{agent_id, hostname, package_count}`. Every response is `{items, next_cursor}` with at most `limit` items or groups, in the #83 (b) order; a cursor holds only ids (package and agent), so its length never depends on stored text (#89). | (1) On a fixture fleet (at least 20 hosts on two OS versions, with tags, multi-arch packages, an epoch version, a `~` version and a `deinstall ok config-files` package), each filter alone, every pair of filters and one five-filter combination return exactly the independently computed set. (2) `version_op` with each of the five operators over at least 50 version pairs taken from the M1 `dpkg --compare-versions` fixture agrees with dpkg (for example `1:1.0` is greater than `2.0`, `1.0~rc1` less than `1.0`); a planted text comparison turns the test red. (3) `status` omitted excludes `deinstall ok config-files` rows and `any` includes them. (4) Each shape has exactly its keys; `host_count` equals the number of distinct hosts holding that name, version and architecture under the same filters, and `package_count` the number of matching rows of that host. (5) Paging N + 1 items or groups with `limit=N` returns each exactly once in the #83 (b) order, for all three shapes, also when two versions are equal in Debian order but differ as text (`1.0` and `1.0-0`), and when a 255-character non-ASCII package name and hostname sit on a page boundary. (6) An empty result is `{"items": [], "next_cursor": null}`. | manager |
| REQ-115 | Package search access and safety | TS §6.2 (L1117), §6 (L1056), §6.3 (L1136, L1141), §8.1 (L1252); security invariants "Data handling", "Authorisation" | Both package routes need at least the viewer role with a `bsu_` or `bsk_` token; agent tokens are refused (TS §6). SQL is built only from fixed fragments with every value a bound parameter; `LIKE` wildcards in `q` and `host` are escaped so they match literally. Stored text is returned verbatim. The routes write nothing and are subject to the 600-per-minute user limit. | (1) No token, a `bsa_` and a `bse_` token get 401; a viewer `bsk_` and a viewer session get 200. (2) `q=%_\'";--` and `host=_` match only names or hostnames that contain those characters literally, with no SQL error; a planted unescaped `%` turns the test red. (3) G1 (ruff S608) passes on `repositories/packages.py`, and a planted f-string query turns it red. (4) A package name and a hostname of hostile text come back byte for byte as JSON strings. ### Changes to earlier requirements (M5)

- REQ-081 (2): the item fields are unchanged; `status` is now typed as the six-value enum (REQ-117).
- REQ-088 (1): the route walk also allows the two REQ-118 static routes (`GET /`, `GET /assets/{name}`), which take no input that reaches the database and are not in the OpenAPI document.
- REQ-089 (1): superseded at the M5 head by REQ-136.
- REQ-090: the README's API-only enrolment path (#37) stays, and the UI path is added by REQ-135.
- REQ-014 (TASK-005 lint): unchanged. The "built key" rule also flags ordinary computed keys such as ``m[`row-${id}`]`` (TASK-005 review nit, carried to M5): M5 code uses a `Map` or a variable key instead, and the rule is not narrowed.

## M6 first-release gate

Planned 2026-10-26 (planner-m6) in the worktree `Bitscan-m6` (branch `m6-release`, from main `8ad50eb`, which holds M0 to M5). Plans: `work/plans/M6-e2e.md` (environment, sequence, stop rule), `work/plans/TASK-131.md`, `work/plans/TASK-133.md` (install layout, shared with TASK-134), `work/plans/TASK-135.md` (release workflow, shared with TASK-136). Open items: #90–#96 (new), the M6 re-check of #6, #28, #46, #89 and the two `.claude/` own-question markers, PD-31, PD-32 (`work/open-questions.md`). Every one has a recommended answer; none of them adds a spec deviation.

Scope, from the roadmap (`work/status.md`: "M6 first-release gate: enrol, approve, heartbeat, inventory on real hosts; inventory alerts and metrics; security subset; release step") and the items carried by the M4 and M5 gate reviews:
- **the manager install method** (#6, install half): a wheel that carries the built UI, an install runbook and an upgrade and operations runbook that reproduce TS §8.7 exactly, and a test that the verbatim manager units run the real binary;
- **the release step** (TS §3.1 L165, L177; §8.1 L1258): version 0.1.0 (#90), a release workflow that builds reproducibly, writes `SHA256SUMS` and SBOMs and creates a draft release whose page carries the SHA-256 values (#91, #92);
- **the end-to-end gate on real hosts** (TS §8.3 L1281, inventory half): the two Hyper-V VMs `bitscan-u2204` and `bitscan-u2404` (Ubuntu 22.04 and 24.04, amd64, cgroup v2; `work/m6-vms.md`) from their `clean` checkpoints, against the manager installed by the runbook (#93);
- **alerts and metrics on real hosts** (TS §8.4): clock skew, runner helper, machine-id mismatch, re-enrolment approved, stale inventory, settings change (#94);
- **the security subset on real hosts** (TS §8.3 L1283);
- **carried items:** the agent idle cost (CHK-M4-266, #95, recorded with no gate), #28 and the N / N−1 marker before the first supported release, #89 (M3 cursors that hold stored text), the reviewer's TASK-004 lint residuals (`fcgi.Serve`, `net.FileConn`, `net.FilePacketConn`).

**Wave 0 is the VM network check** (TP-M6-01). It proves, with a real TLS listener, that each VM reaches the manager's address on TCP 8443, and that the tester reaches each VM. If it fails, every item that needs VM → manager reachability (REQ-146 to REQ-149) stops and waits for the user, with no tunnel, port forward, firewall or admin change (decisions 2026-10-26, overnight rule (4)). No implementation task needs that reachability.

Not in M6: the 10,000-agent load test and every other long-running load or soak test, the two M3 cost checks tied to it (TASK-060, TASK-064), the CSV memory check and any timing of the TS §7 search target (user, 2026-10-24: deferred until after M6); the nightly scheduling of the end-to-end run and its import → scan → results half (M7 to M11; nightly in M11); the runner, sandbox, spool and capread behaviour (M10); any manager behaviour change other than REQ-143; Debian 12 and arm64 (dropped from v1, decisions 2026-10-24); anything in the CLAUDE.md out-of-scope list (no signed APT repository, no signing, no client certificates, no OIDC).

**No migration and no OpenAPI change.** REQ-143 keeps every route's request and response shapes; only the opaque cursor content changes.

Conventions for this section. L… are lines of `docs/spec/Technical_Specification.md`. "The manager host" is the host REQ-138 installs (#93: a fresh systemd-enabled Ubuntu 24.04 container in WSL, published on TCP 8443). "The VMs" are `bitscan-u2204` and `bitscan-u2404` restored from `clean`. "The release candidate" (RC) is the set of REQ-141 files built from the M6 head. "The test doubles" are the tester's OpenBao Transit fake and HTTPS audit sink (#93); they stand in for systems outside Bitscan and are never part of a product file. "Alert entry" means an `audit_log` row with `detail.alert = true` and `detail.alert_kind` as #71 names it.

### Changes to earlier requirements (M6)

- REQ-081 (2) and REQ-082: the cursor of `GET /agents` and `/agents/{id}/packages` now carries only ids (REQ-143); order, items and limits are unchanged.
- REQ-103 (6): the idle cost is now measured on a real host (REQ-149).
- REQ-109: "Publication on a release page from another origin is the M6 release step" is REQ-141.
- REQ-110: the runbook's "0.x is a pre-release" note becomes the 0.1.0 status (REQ-142).
- REQ-135: the README's install and status lines change (REQ-145).
- REQ-147 (4a), (6), (7): the end-to-end run uses one VM at a time (decisions 2026-10-26, "W4 runs one VM at a time"), so these three are checked as `work/plans/M6-e2e.md` L99–L102 says; the requirement text above is unchanged.
## M7 compliance catalog (`.bit` importer and validator)

Planned 2026-10-27 (planner-m7) in the worktree `Bitscan-m7` (branch `m7-catalog`, from main `8ad50eb`, which holds M0 to M5). M6 (REQ-137 to REQ-149, TASK-129 to TASK-138, questions to #97, gates to G53, PD-32) is finishing in `Bitscan-m6` and is not merged; this section continues its numbering and is appended at the end so it merges after M6's section. Summary for the user: `work/plans/M7-breakdown.md`. Open items: #98 to #111 (new), the M7 re-check of #1, #2, #9, #10, #11, #13 to #17, #20, #21, #23, #27, and PD-33 to PD-36 (`work/open-questions.md`, section "M7"). Every one carries a recommended answer; none of the recommendations is a spec deviation.

Scope (lead's M7 brief, 2026-10-27): TS §4.1 import pipeline, §4.2 automatic and manual loading, §4.3 profiles, §4.6 loading `.bit` files; the whole of SR (§1 structure, §2 elements, §3 database mapping and the §3.6 algorithm, §4 validation and BIT102); the TS §5.2 catalog tables and grants (already migrated); the TS §6.2 routes for policies, imports, rebuild, tasks and `DELETE /policies/{id}`; TS §6.3 `.bit` size cap and multipart import; TS §8.1 import controls and parser threats; TS §8.3 importer tests and `bits/tests/` fixtures; TS §8.4 import alerts; TS §8.7 `bitscan-manager bits rebuild`; `bits/samples/`; the catalog screens of the web UI (PD-34). Carried into M7: #1 (sink gate on `DELETE /policies/{id}`), `bits rebuild` (M1 and M2 coverage rows), the `.bit` size cap (M2 TS §6.3 row) and the multipart import (M2 TS §6.3 row).

Not in M7: the evaluator and scoring (TS §4.5, M8; M7 only fixes the number grammar and the RE2 compile helper that BIT012 and BIT013 share with it); scans, schedules, job building and the "refused for disabled or deleted policy" rule (TS §4.4, M9); the runner and the sandbox half of TS §8.3 Sandbox (M10); results and the import → scan → results end-to-end step (M11); signing, an approval or diff workflow and a policy editor (owner decisions); anything in the CLAUDE.md out-of-scope list.

**Migrations: none.** Every table, view and grant M7 uses is already migrated (`m0001_catalog.py`: `family`, `policy`, `plugin`, `policy_plugin`, `bit_import`; `m0004_system.py`: `task`; `m0003_scanning.py`: `scan`, `scan_job`, `scan_schedule`; `m0005_privileges_seeds.py`: `v_plugin` and the TS §5.2 L992–L1013 grants). The schema-diff test stays green; no index may be added. M7 code uses only those grants: INSERT and UPDATE on `policy`, `family`, `plugin`; INSERT and DELETE on `policy_plugin`; INSERT only on `bit_import`; INSERT and UPDATE on `task`; UPDATE of `state` and `finished_at` on `scan_job`; UPDATE on `scan_schedule`; never DELETE on `policy`.

Conventions for this section. L… are lines of `docs/spec/Technical_Specification.md`; SR L… are lines of `docs/spec/bit_Schema_Reference.md`. "MiB" follows #57 (5 MiB = 5,242,880 bytes). "Refused" on a user route means 400 `invalid_request` (#48) unless stated. "The folders" are `BITSCAN_BITS_DIR` (default `/var/lib/bitscan/bits`, the server folder) and its `uploaded/` subfolder. "Fixture" means a file under `bits/tests/` (tester-owned). "Alert entry" means an `audit_log` row with `detail.alert = true` and `detail.alert_kind` as #101 names it. Every requirement that writes holds REQ-161 (the catalog lock).

| REQ | Title | Spec ref | Statement | Acceptance criteria | Area |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| REQ-150 | Safe parse and read-once (BIT001) | TS §4.1 (L516–L517), §8.1 (L1251), §6.3 (L1138); SR §4.1 BIT001 (SR L256); #57, #105 | The importer reads each file into memory once (an upload: the request bytes; a folder file: one read through a descriptor opened with `O_NOFOLLOW`), hashes and parses that buffer, and never re-reads the file. It parses only with `defusedxml`, with DTDs, entity declarations and external references forbidden, at most 5 MiB, UTF-8 only, within the #105 element and depth caps. Any failure is BIT001, and then no content rule runs. | (1) Each fixture gets exactly one BIT001 finding and no other code: an external entity with `file://` and with `http://`, a parameter entity, billion laughs, quadratic blow-up, a DTD with no entity, UTF-16 with BOM, `encoding="ISO-8859-1"`, invalid UTF-8 bytes, not well-formed XML, an empty file, a file of 5 MiB + 1 byte; none of them opens a file or a connection (a canary file's access time and a local listener show no access) and peak memory stays under 64 MiB. (2) A valid file of exactly 5,242,880 bytes passes BIT001. (3) A folder file over 5 MiB is hashed in one streaming read, recorded as BIT001 and never parsed. (4) A file changed on disk after it was read is not read again: the recorded hash and the stored content both come from the first read. (5) A symlink named `*.bit` in either folder is never opened. (6) G1 is red on a planted `import xml.etree.ElementTree` or `import lxml` under `services/importer/`. | manager |

(3) With `status_overridden` true and `policy_status` `disabled`, a file with `enabled` changes `file_status` only; with the override false, both. (4) Reordering plugins changes `sort_order` only (updated 0); removing a plugin retires it and keeps its row (retired 1); changing one `Execute` gives updated 1 and a new `execute_sha256`. (5) A fault injected after the third plugin's upsert leaves every catalog table identical to before (row checksums). (6) A deleted policy re-imported comes back with the same `policy_id`, `deleted_at` NULL, `version` + 1 even for identical bytes and its plugins `active` (#14). (3) With an uploaded policy `X`, copying a server file named `X` moves `X` to it, deletes `uploaded/policy-<id>.bit` and writes one move entry; a later upload named `X` gets 409 `policy_managed_on_server`. (4) A rename inside `a.bit` keeps `policy_id`, plugins and the `scan` rows that reference it; the #106 collision cases behave as #106 says. (5) A file listing a plugin id owned by another policy that lists it gets BIT015 naming that policy; after the owner is deleted, or drops the id, the same file imports and `owner_policy_id` moves to it. ### Changes to earlier requirements (M7)

- REQ-037 (M2 body cap): the `bit` cap class (5 MiB, `app.py` `BODY_CAP_CLASSES`) gets its first route, `POST /api/v1/policies/import`, with the #99 envelope allowance; the 1 MiB default for every other route is unchanged.
- REQ-088 (pull-only and mandatory-inventory invariants, route walk): the walk also allows the eight M7 operations (`GET /policies`, `GET`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /policies/{id}`, `POST /policies/import`, `POST /policies/rebuild`, `GET /tasks/{id}`, `GET /imports`); none of them queues a job or disables inventory.
- REQ-055 (alert delivery) and REQ-086 (metrics): the M7 alert kinds and the import-failure counter of #101 are added; no existing kind or metric changes.
- REQ-133 (navigation): the M7 entries are added (REQ-183).

## M8 evaluator (result evaluation and scoring)

Planned 2026-10-29 (planner-m8) in the worktree `Bitscan-m8` (branch `m8-evaluator`, from main `67a6dfa`, M7 merged). Numbering continues after M7 (REQ-185, TASK-175, #111, PD-36, G61). Summary for the user: `work/plans/M8-breakdown.md`. Open items: #112 to #120 (new) and PD-38, plus #11, #12 and #13 reused as agreed (`work/open-questions.md`, section "M8"). Every new item carries a recommended answer; none of them is a spec deviation.

Scope (lead's M8 brief, 2026-10-29): TS §4.5 as a manager service in `services/evaluator/`: the pure `evaluate()`; the normaliser; the 14 operators exactly; the ERROR rules and the `eval_detail` values; the host compliance score; the rule that picks the latest re-evaluation over the original; and the re-evaluation computation against the policy's current version. Also the pure pieces TS §4.5 defines that M9 and M11 only call: the `eval_spec` contract (L587, L603), the intake verdict with the runner-error and server-side-cap rules (L593, L599, #12), and the read-time results and score queries (L601, L602).

Not in M8, with the milestone (uncovered by design): result intake (`POST /agent/{id}/jobs/{job_id}/results`), storing a result "with its verdict in one insert", the snapshots `plugin_name_snap`, `execute_snap`, `operator_snap`, `expected_snap` (L603), first-result-wins under concurrent retries, late results, the job states and building `eval_spec` when a job is queued (all **M9**); the Prometheus "plugin ERROR rate" metric (TS §8.4 L1288, counted where results arrive: **M9**); the TS §8.3 Integration item "verdict uses the queued `eval_spec` after a catalog change" end to end through intake (**M9**; M8 proves the API-level half, REQ-204); the runner side of the 64 KB / 16 KB truncation and the `truncated` flag (TS §3.5 L251, **M10**, #113); `POST /scans/{id}/reevaluate` with its admin TOTP-session check and sink gate, `GET /scans/{id}` and `/results(.csv)` with `result` and `original_result`, the "n/a" display and the error count beside the score, and any host-page score across policies (TS §6.2 L1120–L1123, §4.5 L601–L602: **M11**).

**Migrations: none.** `scan`, `scan_job`, `scan_result` (and its partitions), `scan_result_reeval` and the grants are already migrated (`m0003_scanning.py`, `m0005_privileges_seeds.py`). M8 code uses only SELECT on the scanning tables, INSERT on `scan_result_reeval` and `audit_log`, and the advisory lock functions; never UPDATE or DELETE on `scan_result` or `scan_result_reeval` (TS §5.2 L1005–L1006). No index may be added (the schema-diff test stays green). **No route, no agent change, no new dependency** (`google-re2` and `hypothesis` are already locked).

Conventions for this section. L… are lines of `docs/spec/Technical_Specification.md`; SR L… are lines of `docs/spec/bit_Schema_Reference.md`. A *verdict* is the pair (`eval_code`, `eval_detail`). "Normalised" means REQ-187. "KB" follows #57 (64 KB = 65,536 bytes). "Owner-written" fixture rows are written by the test as `bitscan_owner`, since no M8 code writes scans, jobs or results. In this section `bm/` means `manager/bitscan_manager/`.

| REQ-186 | `evaluate()` contract | TS §4.5 (L587–L593, L600); SR §2.4 (SR L126, SR L145–L149); `.claude/rules/manager/evaluator.md`; #12, #112 | `bm/services/evaluator/` exposes the pure function `evaluate(operator, expected, stdout, exit_code, timed_out, truncated)`, with exactly these six parameters, returning 0 (PASS), 1 (FAIL) or 2 (ERROR) and nothing else, and `classify()` with the same parameters returning that code together with its `eval_detail` (#112); `evaluate()` returns `classify(...).code`, so there is one comparison implementation. Both are total: any `str` operator, `str` expected, `str` stdout, `int \| None` exit code and two booleans give a verdict and never an exception. They do no I/O, read no clock, log nothing and keep no state. The runner error and the server-side caps are decided before them (#12, REQ-194). | (1) For every row of the REQ-188 to REQ-192 tables, `evaluate(...) == classify(...).code` and the code is 0, 1 or 2. (2) `inspect.signature(evaluate)` has exactly the six parameters, in that order. (3) Property test, at least 10,000 generated inputs (unknown operators, empty strings, lone surrogates, exit codes from −2³¹ to 2³¹−1 and None): no exception, code in {0, 1, 2}, detail None exactly when the code is 0 or 1. (4) Two calls with the same inputs give the same verdict, and neither writes a log record or to stderr, nor opens a file or socket (caplog, capsys and a `sys.addaudithook` probe for `open` and `socket.connect`). | manager |

Nothing else changes: no case folding, no Unicode normalisation, no change to interior whitespace or to a lone CR inside the text. Comparison is case-sensitive. | (1) `"install ok installed\r\n"`, `"\r\n  install ok installed \n"` and `"install ok installed"` normalise to the same text; `"a\r\nb"` gives `"a\nb"`; `"a\rb"` is unchanged; `" x"` and `"x "` keep U+00A0 and U+2003 (#115); `"\v\fx\t"` gives `"x"`. (2) Idempotent only for inputs with no CR directly before a CRLF (decisions 2026-10-29, "Normaliser: one-pass CRLF → LF"): `normalise(normalise(s)) == normalise(s)` for 10,000 generated strings drawn without that pattern. With it, the single literal pass is not idempotent: `normalise("a\r\r\nb") == "a\r\nb"`, and normalising that again gives `"a\nb"`. (3) `equals` with stdout `Install ok installed` against `install ok installed` is FAIL. (4) CRLF inputs are built in code (`.claude/rules/testing.md`), not read from LF-normalised fixture files. ### Changes to earlier requirements (M8)

- REQ-153 (M7 shared value helpers): the evaluator imports `values.parse_number`, `values.compile_pattern` and the `importer.model` operator tuples unchanged; `values.py` and `model.py` are not edited.
- REQ-088 (route walk): unchanged; M8 adds no route.
- The M3 Results contract (`ResultItem`, `api/agent/schemas.py`) is unchanged; M8 only reads its six result fields through a protocol. Whether `stdout` and `stderr` refuse U+0000 and lone surrogates stays with M9 (TASK-048 ruling, "until M9").

## M9 scan engine (jobs, scans, schedules, result intake)

Planned 2026-10-30 (planner-m9) in the worktree `Bitscan-m9` (branch `m9-scan-engine`, from main `d09c537`, M8 merged). Numbering continues after M8 (REQ-205, TASK-185, #120, PD-38, G65). Summary for the user: `work/plans/M9-breakdown.md`. Open items: #121 to #129 (new) and PD-39, PD-40, plus #3, #18, #22, #12, #116, #117, #54 (c), #66 and the two own-question markers (`jobs-and-scans.md:40`, `api.md:44`) reused or now carried as numbers (`work/open-questions.md`, section "M9"). Every new item carries a recommended answer; none of them is a spec deviation.

Scope (lead's M9 brief, 2026-10-30): the manager side that turns scans into jobs and results into stored verdicts. Building compliance jobs from the active plugins of enabled, non-deleted policies (one per resolved host, with `pickup_by`, `redaction_patterns` and M8's `eval_spec` written at queue time); targets resolved at queue time and the recorded policy version; manual and scheduled scans (APScheduler cron with time zones, enable and disable, no back-fill); manager-side cancel; the job state machine and deadlines, with `offline_sweep` extended (deadline fail or partial, the 24 h never-started rule, the single retry); result intake (verdict and result in one insert through M8's `result_verdict()`, first result wins across original and retry, late results for 7 days, 409 for cancelled jobs, `oversize` stored with empty streams, snapshots); the scan-finished rule and the scan state; the threshold alerts of `jobs-and-scans.md` and the "plugin ERROR rate" metric; and the routes these need: the agent job pull for compliance jobs and the results post (TS §6.1), `POST /scans`, `POST /scans/{id}/cancel` and CRUD `/scan-schedules` (TS §6.2).

Not in M9, with the milestone (uncovered by design): the agent runner, its sandbox, redaction and truncation (**M10**; M9 tests post simulated agent requests built from the shared contract fixtures in `manager/tests/contract/fixtures/`). The read views `GET /scans` (L1118), `GET /scans/{id}` (L1120, "scan state + per-host summary", which needs M8's scores), `GET /scans/{id}/results` and `/results.csv` (L1121), `GET /agents/{id}/scans` (L1111), `POST /scans/{id}/reevaluate` (L1123), the scan and schedule screens, CSV exports, the score display and the nightly end-to-end run (**M11**). These routes are M11's because no M9 acceptance test needs them: M9 observes scan and job state through the create and cancel answers, the services and `bitscan_app` reads, while every route M9's own tests drive (create, cancel, schedules, pull, results) is in M9. Retention of scans (TS §5.3 L1047) stays with the retention task (M11).

**Migrations: none.** `scan_schedule`, `scan`, `scan_job`, `scan_result` (with its partitions) and the grants are already migrated (`m0003_scanning.py`, `m0005_privileges_seeds.py`). M9 uses INSERT on `scan`, `scan_job`, `scan_result` and `audit_log`; UPDATE only of `scan_job` (state, dispatched_at, started_at, deadline_at, finished_at), `scan` (state, finished_at) and `scan_schedule`; DELETE only on `scan_schedule`; `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` and `FOR SHARE` where these grants allow it; advisory transaction locks. Never UPDATE or DELETE on `scan_result`, and never a write to `payload`, `eval_spec`, `agent_id`, `scan_id` or `retry_of` of an existing job (TS §5.2 L1005–L1013). No index is added (the schema-diff test stays green; #127 (f) bounds the full reads this costs). **No agent change, no contract change** (the TS §6.5 schemas and fixtures stay as frozen in M3; #121 keeps the NUL fixture valid), **no new dependency** (APScheduler 3.11, `tzdata` on Windows, `google-re2` and `hypothesis` are already locked).

Conventions for this section. L… are lines of `docs/spec/Technical_Specification.md`. A *unit* is one host's original job together with its retry, if any (#127 (d)). "Has a result" means #127 (a). "Clock control" means the runtime clock is a test double moved by the test, never `sleep` (decisions 2026-10-26). "Simulated agent" means requests built from the contract fixtures and sent with a real `bsa_` token through the real routes. In this section `bm/` means `manager/bitscan_manager/`.

| REQ | Title | Spec ref | Statement | Acceptance criteria | Area |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| REQ-206 | Job state rules (pure) | TS §4.4 (L562–L575, L578, L582), §3.3 (L222); #124 (a), #127 (a)–(c) | `bm/services/jobs/states.py` holds the job state rules as pure functions over plain values: the allowed transitions (`queued → dispatched \| expired \| cancelled`; `dispatched → running \| failed \| cancelled`; `running → completed \| partial \| failed \| cancelled`; `failed \| partial → completed \| partial`); the deadline `started + ceil(Σ timeout_s ÷ max_parallel_plugins) s + 600 s`; the outcome of an accepted batch and of a passed deadline (#127 (a), (b)); whether a job accepts results at a given time (#124 (a)); and the final states `completed`, `expired`, `cancelled`. It does no I/O and reads no clock. | (1) A table test of every (state, event) pair (pull, batch empty or not, final or not, every plugin with a result or not, deadline passed, 24 h passed, cancel, pickup passed) gives the #127 answer, and every pair the table does not allow is refused. (2) Deadline: Σ 95 s with 2 parallel gives start + 648 s; Σ 30 s with 8 gives start + 604 s; Σ 3,000,000 s (5,000 × 600) with 1 is exact (integer arithmetic); a `max_parallel_plugins` outside 1–8 or an empty plugin list raises `ValueError`. (3) Property test: no event leaves `completed`, `expired` or `cancelled`; `failed` and `partial` only reach `completed` or `partial`; `running` is never re-entered. (4) Acceptance window: `dispatched_at` + 7 days exactly is accepted and 1 µs later refused; `queued`, `expired`, `cancelled` and inventory are refused at any time; `completed` is accepted (it yields only duplicates). (5) The module imports nothing from `db`, `repositories`, `api` or psycopg (import-graph test). | manager |
| REQ-207 | Scan state derivation (pure) | TS §4.4 (L579–L580); #22, #127 (d), (e); `.claude/rules/manager/jobs-and-scans.md:40` | `bm/services/jobs/scan_state.py` derives a scan's state and whether it is finished from its units (#127 (d)): any open unit gives `queued` (every job still `queued`) or `running`; otherwise `completed` (every unit complete), `partial` (some unit has results), `cancelled` (no results and some job cancelled) or `failed`. It never gives `dispatched` or `expired`. Pure. | (1) Table: every job queued → `queued`, not finished; one job dispatched → `running` (#22); one expired and one queued → `running`; every unit complete → `completed`, finished; an original `partial` whose retry completed the missing plugins → that unit complete; one unit complete and one failed without results → `partial`; no results and one job cancelled → `cancelled`; no results, nothing cancelled (expired, failed) → `failed`; cancelled after some results → `partial` (or `completed` when every unit was complete). (2) Property test over random unit sets: finished exactly when no unit is open; the state is never `dispatched` or `expired`; adding a result never turns `partial` or `completed` into `failed` or `cancelled`. (3) PostgreSQL as `bitscan_app`: with agents in each of the six statuses, `{"all": true}` resolves to the `enrolled`, `online`, `stale` and `offline` ones; `{"tags": ["a", "b"]}` to the eligible agents holding `a` or `b`; `{"agents": [x, unknown, decommissioned]}` to `[x]`. (2) No payload holds an `operator`, `expected`, `expected_result`, `eval_spec` or `name` key, at any depth. Across DST it follows the user's decision (decisions 2026-10-30, "DST behaviour for scheduled scans"): a run in the spring gap is shifted to just after the missing hour, and a repeated local time fires once (a second occurrence of the same local wall time is dropped). (3) Refused: six fields, `@daily`, both day of month and day of week restricted (`0 0 1 * 1`), 65 characters, `0 0 30 2 *` (never fires), `61 * * * *`; time zones `Mars/Base`, `localtime`, `../etc/passwd`, 65 characters. ### Changes to earlier requirements (M9)

- **REQ-072 (job pull):** criterion (4) ("a compliance job … is not returned in M3") is superseded by REQ-213; (1), (2), (3), (5), (6) still hold. The tester rewrites only that check (PD-40).
- **REQ-073 (results route in M3):** superseded by REQ-214 to REQ-217 for compliance jobs: well-formed posts for an accepting compliance job are now stored. Kept unchanged: the 409 for unknown, foreign and inventory job ids, the refusal audit entry (2), 400 and 413 (3), the flood alert (4, with the #124 (f) exclusion) and no stdout in logs (5) (PD-40).
- **REQ-078 (`offline_sweep`):** extended by REQ-218 and REQ-219; its M3 steps and their independence are unchanged (its docstring's "the compliance steps … come with M9").
- **REQ-079 (fleet alerts):** unchanged code; REQ-228 proves it with compliance jobs.
- **REQ-086 (metrics):** one counter added (REQ-226); every label still bounded.
- **REQ-088 (route walk):** the walk now includes the seven M9 user operations (`POST /scans`, `POST /scans/{id}/cancel`, and the five `/scan-schedules` operations), all TS §6.2 routes; no other route is added.
- **TASK-048 ruling (stdout and stderr NUL, "until M9"):** answered by #121 without a contract change; `results/request_valid_nul_in_stdout_and_stderr.json` stays valid.
- **REQ-195, REQ-194, REQ-198 (M8 `eval_spec`, `result_verdict`, results repository):** used unchanged; M9 is their first writer and caller.

## M10 agent runner and sandbox

Planned 2026-10-30 (planner-m10) in the worktree `Bitscan-m10` (branch `m10-runner`, from main `328b097`, M9 merged). Numbering continues after M9 (REQ-233, TASK-208, #129, PD-40, G70). Summary for the user: `work/plans/M10-breakdown.md`. Open items: #130 to #143 (new) and PD-41 to PD-45, plus #4, #28, #29, #30, #31, #57, #60, #74, #78, #113, #114, #116, #121 and #124 (f) reused as agreed and the own-question marker `runner-sandbox.md:43` (now carried as #133) (`work/open-questions.md`, section "M10"). Every new item carries a recommended answer; none of them is a spec deviation.

Scope (lead's M10 brief, 2026-10-30): everything that makes compliance jobs run on a host. Agent side: hand each accepted compliance job whole to the runner over `runner.sock`, receive its results, post them in batches (start notice, late results, final), spool them while the manager cannot be reached (50 MB, oldest dropped as `spool_overflow`), report `runner_unavailable`, keep the M4 job checks, and leave inventory exactly as it is (G2). Runner: the socket and its peer check, the job re-checks, process hygiene, the plugin environment, `/bin/sh -c`, cgroup v2 supervisor and per-plugin cgroups with `cgroup.kill` and a subtree sweep, the refusal to start without unified cgroup v2, and what is captured per plugin. Redaction on the host before truncation. The capread helper (server and client). The real binaries in the `.deb` against the verbatim units. The Unit, Agent, Sandbox and Security tests of TS §8.3 for these, with planted-fault builds.

Not in M10 (uncovered by design, with the milestone): the results UI, `POST /scans/{id}/reevaluate`, the CSV exports, the read views and the nightly end-to-end run inventory → import → scan → results on three VMs (**M11**; M10 has one gate walk on one VM, REQ-263); the N / N−1 agent–manager matrix (**M11**); TASK-146 (the BIT102 review and r2) is a named carry-over for a fresh session and is not planned into a wave. Debian 12 and arm64 are not supported (owner decisions 2026-10-24, recorded deviations from TS §3.1, §8.3): nothing in M10 builds, tests or emulates them. **No manager change:** the agent contract (TS §6.5, `manager/openapi.json`, the shared fixtures) stays as frozen in M3 and used by M9, and no migration, route or setting is added. **No unit change:** the TS §3.7 units and D-Bus policy stay verbatim (with the recorded `RemoveOnStop=yes` exception); if a VM run shows that a unit must change, that is a spec question for the user, never a fix.

Conventions for this section. L… are lines of `docs/spec/Technical_Specification.md`. `ag/` means `agent/`. Sizes follow #57 (1 MiB = 1,048,576 bytes; 64 KB = 65,536; 16 KB = 16,384; 1.5 MB = 1,572,864). *Sandbox host* means a host running the built `.deb` with the verbatim units: the Hyper-V VMs `bitscan-u2204` and `bitscan-u2404`, or a GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-22.04` or `ubuntu-24.04` runner (PD-44). *Driver* means the tester's program that plays the agent on `runner.sock` as uid `bitscan`. *Probe* means the tester's static test binary installed under `/usr/local/bin` on a sandbox host (an allowed execution path) to make system calls a shell cannot. *Refused job* means the runner answers `refused` with a reason word and starts no process (an exec counter in unit builds, a marker file on sandbox hosts). *Runner unavailable* is defined by #131 (a).

| REQ-234 | Agent–runner protocol (interface) | TS §3.7 (L279–L282), §3.5 (L248–L249, L252), §6.5 (L1165–L1184); #29, #60, #130 | `ag/internal/runner/proto` defines the only messages on `runner.sock`. One request per connection: `{"v": 1, "job": <TS §6.5 compliance Job>, "max_parallel_plugins": 1–8}`. Replies: `accepted`, `refused` (with a #130 reason word), `result` (one TS §6.5 Results item) and `done`. A frame is a 4-byte big-endian length then one compact JSON object: at most 32 MiB for the request and 1 MiB for a reply. Decoding is strict (unknown or missing keys, trailing data, wrong types and the #60 bounds are refused), and a result item must also fit 65,536 stdout and 16,384 stderr UTF-8 bytes. No message carries a command except inside a whole job. Pure Go. | (1) Every frame type round-trips to equal values; a job decoded from the contract fixtures under `manager/tests/contract/fixtures/jobs/` is carried unchanged. (2) Refused at decode, without reading an oversize frame whole: an unknown key at any level (for example `"command"` beside `"job"`), `v` other than 1, a missing `max_parallel_plugins`, 0 or 9, an inventory job, a frame of 32 MiB + 1 byte (request) or 1 MiB + 1 byte (reply), a zero-length frame, a truncated frame, two JSON values in one frame. (3) A result item with stdout of 65,537 UTF-8 bytes, stderr of 16,385, or an `error` other than null, `spool_overflow` and `runner_unavailable` is refused by both `NewResult` and the reply decoder. (4) An API listing test: the package exports no function or type that takes a command string other than a whole `wire.Job`. (5) Tests run on Windows and Linux. | agent |
| REQ-235 | Socket activation and peer check | TS §3.7 (L281–L283, L289–L302, L486–L493), §2.2 (L111); security invariants "Never add"; #130 | `ag/internal/sdsock` (used by the runner and the capread helper) takes the listening socket only from systemd: `LISTEN_PID` equal to its own pid, `LISTEN_FDS` = 1, fd 3 a listening `AF_UNIX` stream socket. It marks fd 3 and every other inherited descriptor above 2 close-on-exec before anything else, never creates or binds a socket, and accepts a connection only when the peer's `SO_PEERCRED` uid equals the uid it was given (the runner: user `bitscan`; capread: user `bitscan-runner`, each looked up at start). The runner serves one connection at a time (#130 (g)). | (1) Unit with a Unix socket in a temporary directory and an injected expected uid: the expected uid is accepted; any other uid (0 included) is closed before any byte is read, with one log line naming the uid and nothing else. (2) A missing or foreign `LISTEN_PID`, `LISTEN_FDS` ≠ 1, fd 3 not a socket, a datagram socket or a non-listening socket: start-up refused (exit 1, one line). (3) On a sandbox host: root and a user added to group `bitscan` can connect but are closed without a reply; a user outside the group gets EACCES from the 0660 socket. (4) A second connection while a job runs gets `refused busy`. (5) The runner's fd 3 carries `O_CLOEXEC` (`/proc/<pid>/fdinfo/3` flags), and a plugin sees only descriptors 0, 1 and 2 (REQ-239 (2)). (6) Static (G8): the only `net.FileListener` call is in `internal/sdsock/listen_linux.go`; no non-test file calls `net.Listen*`, `syscall.Socket`/`Bind`/`Listen` or `unix.Socket`/`Bind`/`Listen`. | agent |

| REQ-240 | Cleanup: no plugin outlives its job | TS §3.5 (L250), §3.7 (L283–L284), §8.1 (L1245); #135, #138 | When a plugin exits or reaches its timeout the runner writes `cgroup.kill` for its cgroup, waits for it to empty, removes it and deletes the plugin's temp directory. | REQ-242 | Redaction and truncation | TS §3.5 (L251), §8.1 (L1249), §8.6 (L1324, L1329–L1339); security invariants "Secrets and output"; #113, #116 (a), #133, #134 | `ag/internal/redact` turns one captured stream into what leaves the runner: at most 1 MiB read (the rest drained and discarded), invalid UTF-8 replaced by U+FFFD, the job's patterns applied in list order (Go `regexp`, RE2 syntax; each whole match → `[REDACTED]`, no capture-group expansion), then the built-in cut-off private-key rule (#133), then a cut at a UTF-8 boundary to at most 65,536 bytes (stdout) or 16,384 bytes (stderr). For stdout, `truncated` is true when more than 1 MiB was produced or the redacted text was cut; cutting stderr sets nothing (#113). The growth guard of #134 (f) applies. Pure Go. | (1) A `bsa_` token starting at byte 65,530 of stdout is fully `[REDACTED]`, and no prefix of it appears in the output. (2) A private key whose END line lies beyond the 1 MiB read limit is redacted from its BEGIN line to the end, with and without default pattern 2 in the list (#133). (3) 65,537 ASCII bytes → 65,536 and `truncated` true; 32,769 two-byte runes → at most 65,536 bytes ending on a rune boundary; 16,385 bytes of stderr → 16,384 and `truncated` false. (4) Invalid UTF-8 becomes U+FFFD before matching, and the JSON-encoded string, decoded again, is never over its cap in UTF-8 bytes. (7) Patterns `[a-z]` then `[A-Z]` on 1 MiB of letters trigger the growth guard: a single `[REDACTED]`, `truncated` true, memory bounded. (2) For each failure kind (no socket, connection refused, 30 s without a first reply, `refused <word>`, EOF after 2 of 5 results, an oversize or malformed frame, a result for a plugin not in the job): the received results are posted as received, the missing plugins as `runner_unavailable` in the final batch, the runner gets no second request, and the next heartbeat's `last_error` is the #131 (c) text. ### Changes to earlier requirements (M10)

- **REQ-104 (0.x runner, capread and compliance jobs):** criteria (2) and (3) are superseded by REQ-245, REQ-252 and REQ-253: the runner and capread are no longer idle, and the agent now connects to `runner.sock`. Criterion (1) (binaries and units as REQ-106) still holds. The #74 answer survives as the agent's behaviour when the runner is unavailable (#28, #131).
- **REQ-097 (2) (`spool_bytes` 0 in 0.x):** superseded by REQ-250 (1).
- **REQ-098 (4) (compliance per #74):** superseded by REQ-245; criteria (1)–(3) and (5) still hold (REQ-249).
- **REQ-103 (1) and (5):** unchanged for the agent; the runner's and helper's own execution and sockets are bounded by REQ-235, REQ-251 and REQ-256.
- **REQ-010 (M0 stubs) and REQ-111 (stub-execution step):** the stub check for runner and capread is replaced by REQ-257.
- **REQ-106 (package contents):** same file list; the binaries are the real ones (REQ-255).
- **The M4 tests of the #74 handler (TP-M4-18 compliance rows):** rewritten to REQ-245 by the tester, old → new CHK list in `tests/m10/report.md` (PD-45); every other M4 assertion stays.

## M11 results and end-to-end

Planned 2026-11-08 (planner-m11) in the worktree `Bitscan-m11` (branch `m11-results`, from main `6efe0fb`, M10 closed). Numbering continues after M10 (REQ-263, TASK-231, #143, PD-45 used; G79). Summary for the user: `work/plans/M11-breakdown.md`. Open items: #144 to #154 (new) and PD-46 to PD-51, plus #3, #9, #12, #28, #74, #84, #89, #93, #111, #118, #119, #120, #122, #127 (f) and the `CLAUDE.md` N / N−1 marker reused or carried (`work/open-questions.md`, section "M11"). Every new item carries a recommended answer; none of them is a spec deviation. Two need the user whatever is recommended: the `CLAUDE.md` N / N−1 marker (REQ-288) and #151 (retention cost without indexes, an owner question about the TS §5.2 DDL).

Scope (lead's M11 brief, 2026-11-08, checked against the spec): the read views and results routes of TS §6.2 (`GET /scans`, `GET /scans/{id}` with its per-host summary and score, `GET /scans/{id}/results` and `/results.csv` with `result` and `original_result`, `GET /agents/{id}/scans`), with stdout and stderr for operator and admin only; `POST /scans/{id}/reevaluate` (admin, TOTP session, sink gate) on M8's service; the scan, results and schedule screens of the web UI (TS §2.1 L101, §6.3); the retention task (TS §5.3 L1049, L1053) as `bitscan_retention` through `retention_dsn`, scheduled by the `retention` `system_schedule` row; the nightly end-to-end run on three VMs (TS §8.3 L1283); the N / N−1 compatibility rows (TS §8.2 L1269, §8.7 L1350); the README and runbook carries; and what M11 must leave ready for a release (no tag: the user authorises releases).

Not in M11 (uncovered by design): load tests (deferred by the user); Debian 12 and arm64 (not supported, decisions 2026-10-24); everything out of scope in `CLAUDE.md`; TASK-146 (the BIT102 review, its second approval and r2: a named carry-over, not rescheduled since decisions 2026-10-30); confirming the CIS control numbers in `bits/samples/` (#111, still open: a user question, not a task; the nightly run uses the SR §1.1 canonical example until it is answered, #152 (e)). **No alert is added for re-evaluation**: TS §8.4 L1292–L1293 lists none and #118 (g) was approved as "no alert" (the brief's "alerted" is carried as #148).

**Migrations: none.** Every table, grant and seed row M11 uses exists (`m0003_scanning.py`, `m0004_system.py`, `m0005_privileges_seeds.py`): `bitscan_retention` already holds DELETE on the nine retention tables, INSERT on `audit_log` and USAGE on its sequence (TS §5.2 L1003–L1006), and the `retention` `system_schedule` row is seeded (L834–L835). No index may be added (G19 stays green); #151 records the cost. **No agent change, no contract change, no unit change.** The agent changelog gets the M11 build's pre-release entry only (PD-50).

**Frozen interfaces reused unchanged:** M8's `services/evaluator/score.py` (`host_score`, `HostScore`, `effective`), `scores.py` (`AgentScore`, `scan_host_scores`), `reevaluate.py` (`reevaluate_scan`, `ScanNotFound`, `PolicyDeleted`, `ReevalResult`), `repositories/results.py` (`_EFFECTIVE`, `effective_results`, `host_counts`) and `repositories/reeval.py`; M9's `services/jobs/*` (`ScanState`, `queue_scan`, `cancel_scan`), `repositories/scan_progress.py` and the scan and schedule routes; M2's `require_role`, `require_totp_session`, `page_params`, `encode_cursor`/`decode_cursor`; M5's `services/packages/csv_export.py` (`escape_cell`, `write_csv`). PD-46 records how the new results query reuses `results._EFFECTIVE` without editing `results.py`.

Conventions for this section. L… are lines of `docs/spec/Technical_Specification.md` as of `6efe0fb` (the M10 unit edit moved §3.7 onwards by 2 lines, so earlier milestones' line numbers for §4.4 onwards are 2 lower); SR L… are lines of `docs/spec/bit_Schema_Reference.md`. "Effective verdict" means #119 (the highest `reeval_id`, else the original). "Unit" means #127 (d) (a host's original job and its retry). "Item shapes" are the #144 and #145 answers. "Clock control" means the injected runtime clock, never `sleep` (decisions 2026-10-26). "Canary" means a random marker string placed in stdout, stderr, `execute` and an expected value. In this section `bm/` means `manager/bitscan_manager/`.

| REQ | Title | Spec ref | Statement | Acceptance criteria | Area |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| REQ-264 | Scan list | TS §6.2 (L1120), §6.3 (L1135–L1136, L1141), §4.4 (L557, L562, L582); #89, #145 (a), (d) | `GET /api/v1/scans` (viewer; `bsu_` or `bsk_`) answers `{items, next_cursor}`, newest first (`created_at` descending, then `scan_id` descending), `limit` 1–500 (default 50), the cursor holding the last `scan_id` only (#89). Filters, combined with AND: `policy_id`; `agent_id` (the scan's stored resolved target holds that agent); `state` (one of `queued`, `running`, `completed`, `partial`, `failed`, `cancelled`); `created_after` (inclusive) and `created_before` (exclusive), RFC 3339 with an offset. Each item is the #145 (a) scan item. | (1) PostgreSQL through the API, with owner-written scans of two policies in three states over two days with overlapping targets: each filter alone and all together return exactly the expected scans, in order; `limit=2` paging visits every scan once. (2) A viewer, an operator and an admin get 200 with a session and with an API token; no token, a revoked token and a `bsa_` token get 401. (3) 400 `invalid_request` for an unknown `state`, a malformed `agent_id`, a date without an offset, `limit` 0 or 501, a tampered cursor, a cursor made under other filters, and a cursor whose scan was deleted. (4) No item holds `resolved_agents`, stdout, stderr, `execute` or an expected value. (5) A statement spy sees SELECT only. | manager |

The scan state is read from `scan.state`, never re-derived (M9 interface note). (2) For 50 random scans, every host's counts and score equal M8's `scores.scan_host_scores()`. (3) Unknown id 404 `not_found`; a non-hyphenated id 400. ### Changes to earlier requirements (M11)

- **REQ-080 (scheduler wiring, M3) and the M9 runner checks:** the job set grows from three to four (`retention`, REQ-277). The tester rewrites only the job-set and stop-record checks of `test_scheduler_runner.py` and `test_serve_scheduler_linux.py` under PD-51, listing old and new CHK ids in `tests/m11/report.md`; every other assertion stays.
- **REQ-233 (README, M9):** "results views arrive with M11" is superseded by REQ-289; the README walk checks of that sentence are rewritten under PD-51.
- **REQ-088 (route walk):** now includes the six M11 operations, all TS §6.2 routes; no other route is added.
- **REQ-196 to REQ-201 (M8 score, effective verdict, results repository, host scores, re-evaluation):** used unchanged; M11 is their first route-level caller. `scores.scan_host_scores()` stays the reference that REQ-265 (2) agrees with.
- **REQ-221 and REQ-224 (M9 scan and schedule routes):** unchanged; the M11 screens call them.
